# Backlog

*Open, actionable work items only. Completed work → `CHANGELOG.md`. Feature inventory & plans → `ROADMAP.md`.*

> **⚠ Historical `BACKLOG:NNN` / `BACKLOG item N` references do not resolve — and never reliably did.**
> Sessions used to cite this file by **line number** (`BACKLOG` + colon + a line number) or by the
> roadmap's **item number** (`BACKLOG` + the word *item* + a number). Both are positional: any insertion
> above a cited line silently retargets it at a *different* item, which is worse than a broken link
> because it still looks valid. By Session 150, 12 of the 34 distinct cited line numbers already landed
> on blank or unrelated lines — and one had been wrong from the day it was written.
>
> Session 150 rewrote all **194** such citations in **live** artifacts (`src/`, `test/`, `docs/`, and this
> file) to name their target instead — `CHANGELOG: <title>, Session N` for work that has shipped, or
> `BACKLOG: <item title>` for work still open here. (The replacement text carries no quote or backtick
> on purpose: several citations sit inside `describe(...)`/`it(...)` string literals, where an inserted
> quote terminates the string and breaks the build.) It deliberately left the ~523 occurrences in the
> **append-only history** (`SESSION_NOTES.md`, `HANDOFFS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md`)
> untouched: rewriting those would edit the record of what past sessions actually wrote. **When you meet a
> `BACKLOG:NNN` in a historical document, read it as "some item in `BACKLOG.md` as it stood that day" and
> resolve it with `git show <commit>:BACKLOG.md`, not against the current file.**
>
> **Do not add new positional citations.** Cite items by title.

***A note on this file's history — it has now been drained TWICE, and only the second drain
came with a detector.** It accumulated completed items for ~150 sessions despite its own header
rule and SESSION_RUNNER Phase 3F. Session 150 removed 115 KB (46% of the file) after verifying
each block against `CHANGELOG.md`, and wrote "keep it clean" — an instruction, which is what the
file already had. It re-accumulated: by Session 194 there were **24 completed records, 33 KB,
19% of the file**, from 30 sessions, and that session's own close-out added one. Session 195
removed them and shipped **`check-backlog.js`**, asserted by
`test/unit/backlog-hygiene-gate.test.ts` so it runs on every `npm test`.

**Keep it clean: when a session completes a backlog item, delete it here in the same commit that
records it in the ledger.** ⚠ And when you are tempted to keep the bullet because the item was
filed WRONG and the correction is worth having — that is the exact impulse that refilled this
file twice. The correction belongs in `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md`; see #283.)*

The first read that finds no closer now builds one table from the end of the line over three quote states, and every later `{` looks its closer up. 15 refused-shape rows in `pathological-inputs.test.ts` fail on the parent at 3.9 to 8.6 s against 1,000 ms on the clock alone (the answer each asserts first is right) and take under 30 ms now; the old function is the oracle in `test/unit/brace-groups-equivalence.test.ts` (8.56 million enumerated and seeded strings over three alphabets, 18 stated rows); `tools/mutation-run/mutants/s326-brace-groups*.json` hold the mutants; the review round compared the two on 358 million strings (every string over `{ } " '` up to 14 characters) and 490,000 documents through 18 readers with no difference. **Known residuals** (this part only): the table is sized by the line, 4 bytes a character (135 MB peak for 20,000,000 `{`, 2.4 GB for 500,000,000) and is built at the first failed read, which costs a short line with two stray `{` 35 to 50 ns more (filed in Polish below); `isAttributeBlock`'s prefix test is quadratic in a leading run times the groups on the line (filed below); the `{` run on a HEADING line is `HEADING_ATTRIBUTE`, the Session 318 item's part (2), still open; `test:integration` NOT run. A top-level ```` ```a`b ```` line does the same — pandoc reads a backtick in a backtick fence's info string as no fence at all (CommonMark §4.5), and this model opens one. Mirroring it would need citation resolution, four metadata gates and a "section holds only its heading" test, and would make the Outline HTML-specific.
  - **(3) R3 — a GRID TABLE's cells hold markdown blocks, and NONE of what they hold is an Outline heading.** Quarto renders headings from inside grid-table cells; Posit's Outline shows none of them; the model shows none of them; the decision is to keep it that way. ✅ **This one rests on a BLACK-BOX OBSERVATION, not a bundle grep** — `npm run observe:posit` (Session 270), run with the operator's go-ahead against `quarto.quarto-1.135.0`: the control `## Control Heading` returned `Constant:Control Heading`, `ours-loaded: false`, and all three R3 shapes returned **(no symbols)**. Measured on quarto 1.7.33, the shapes are: an ATX heading at **column 0 of the cell** (`|### x` and `| ### x` render; `|  ### x` at two spaces does not — pandoc's ATX rule is column-exact everywhere, not a cell special case); a **multi-line cell** whose lines fuse into a setext `h1`; and **two independent divider-collapse triggers** — a row whose last non-whitespace character lands **two or more** columns past the border's right edge (⚠ ONE past does not fire), and a `+===+` divider **truncated at a column boundary**. Corpus reach: 63 of 3,383 documents hold a grid border, 3 of them diverge (482 ATX, 491 and 610 fused setext, 8 headings), and the two other documents holding `| ### ` cell lines (304, 615) **quarto refuses to render at all** — 615's cells hold FENCED CODE whose `#` lines are not headings, which is what a naive fix would have phantomed. ⚠ **The residual, disclosed rather than hidden:** 482's author really did type `### Basics` inside a cell and meant a heading; neither oracle shows it, so this decision costs that document 3 Outline rows. Evidence: `scratchpad/s270/` (the probes, `MY-MEASUREMENTS.md`, `posit-observation.txt`), 42 verifying agents of which 9 corrected a claim.
  - **(4) R2 — a `---` pair that becomes valid YAML once knitr has run swallows the heading between them, and the Outline KEEPS it.** `quarto render` renders no heading there; **Posit's Outline shows it; this model shows it; the decision is to keep showing it.** ✅ **Black-box observation, not a bundle grep** — `npm run observe:posit` (Session 271), operator go-ahead taken BEFORE the launch, against `quarto.quarto-1.135.0`: `ours-loaded: false`, both controls green (`Constant:Control Heading` for `.qmd` and `Constant:Control Heading Rmd` for `.Rmd`, so an `.Rmd` row means "no heading", not "no provider"), and Posit returned `Constant:Swallowed Heading` for the shape in BOTH `.Rmd` and `.qmd`, and all five `## Slide N` of a slidify deck where quarto renders three. **Mechanism, measured firsthand on quarto 1.7.33 and wider than the re-score's line:** the outcome is BINARY, and neither branch renders the heading. A chunk that leaves NOTHING behind (`echo=FALSE`, `echo=FALSE, results='hide'`, `include=FALSE`) reduces the span to a YAML comment plus the empty cell div. ⚠ **That is OBSERVED, not inferred** — rendered with `keep-md: true`, the intermediate `.html.md` holds exactly `---` / `## Swallowed Heading` / blank / `::: {.cell}` / blank / `:::` / blank / blank / `---` (`scratchpad/s271/keepmd/`). The heading line is a YAML COMMENT, which is what makes the span parse; ⚠ the cell div is a **passenger, not the cause** — quarto does emit `YAML warning (line 7 column 1): Duplicate key: ['::']` because `::: {.cell}` and `:::` both read as a `::` key, but deleting both lines by hand swallows the heading just the same (`v03_no_celldiv` vs `v04_with_celldiv`, both exit 0, both heading-free). A chunk whose SOURCE or OUTPUT survives (default `echo`, `eval=FALSE`, or a plot under `results='hide'` — `results` does not suppress a figure) makes the span unparseable and **`quarto render` FAILS**: exit 1, `YAML parse exception`, `Error running Lua`. There is no spelling where quarto renders that heading. ⚠ **THREE PRECONDITIONS, all measured, and the first is the one a fix would key on:** (i) **no blank line between the opening `---` and the heading** — insert one and the heading RENDERS (`v01_blank_after_opener`, exit 0, `Swallowed Heading` present), which is exactly why this bites slidify decks, whose convention is `---` immediately above `## Slide Title`; (ii) the span must hold no PROSE — a prose line kills the render at quarto's own pre-knitr metadata reader (`v02_prose_in_span`, exit 1, `Error reading metadata from …`, a different stage from the Lua failure above); (iii) a closing `---` must exist before EOF. ⚠ **So "is the span valid YAML" is the WRONG predicate** — the gate is pandoc's structural `yaml_metadata_block` opener precondition, and YAML validity only chooses which flavour of not-a-heading you get (metadata swallow, a simple-table fallback that puts the heading in a `<td>`, or a hard failure). ⚠ **The cost of matching quarto was measured BEFORE the decision, and it is why the decision went this way:** dropping `FENCE_ANYWHERE_IN_BLOCK` scores *better* — 2,421 → 2,422 of 2,438 — while the document-by-document bundle diff says it removes **90 headings from 32 documents, 31 of which `quarto render` exits 1 on**. They are slidify-era `.Rmd` decks (`framework: io2012`) where every slide boundary is a bare `---` and quarto dies reading a slide as YAML (`bad indentation of a mapping entry`) — so the score can see exactly 1 of the 32, and the Outline would go sparse in precisely the documents where it is the only navigation that works. Evidence: `scratchpad/s271/` (`posit-observation.txt`, `probefiles/`, `probes/`, the `build-nofence` score run). ⚠ **By-catch, filed separately and NOT part of this decision:** the same observation shows the model DELETING a heading Posit keeps on the no-chunk spelling — CLOSED by Session 272; see decision (5) below.
  - **(5) MID-DOCUMENT YAML METADATA IS NOT MODELLED AT ALL — the Outline shows what is inside a `---` span, and `quarto render` shows nothing.** ✅ **Black-box observation, the widest yet** — `npm run observe:posit` (Session 272), operator go-ahead taken BEFORE the launch, against `quarto.quarto-1.135.0`: `ours-loaded: false`, both controls green, and **all 15 probe files rendered through real quarto FIRST and then proved BYTE-IDENTICAL to the bytes the harness writes**, so both oracles answer for the same document. Posit shows the heading for **every** spelling the old rule suppressed — the corpus shape (`Alpha Slide Head`), a real-keys span (`note: bravo`), a keys-plus-heading span (`Charlie Inside`), BOTH block-quote spellings (`note: delta`, `Echo Quoted`, never observed before), two consecutive spans (`Golf One`, `Hotel Two`) and the tight spelling (`Foxtrot Tight`). ⚠ **And the decisive control is `m11_fm_only`:** a front matter carrying `author: Kilo Author` returns ONLY `Only Real Heading`, so Posit is not ignoring YAML — it models **the opening block and only the opening block**, which is exactly what this model does now. **Session 272 FIXED it** (`CHANGELOG.md`): 80 headings recovered across 30 authored documents, 0 lost, score unmoved at 2421 / 2438 with identical ordinal lists, all 30 `.Rmd` decks `quarto render` exits 1 on. ⚠ **Two residuals are NOT part of this decision and are a different axis** — an ATX pressed against a paragraph line is folded in by `blank_before_header`, and an ATX underlined by `---` keeps its literal hashes; Posit shows both clean, this model follows quarto, and **both reproduce outside any `---` span on both builds** (`scratchpad/s272/axis/x01`, `x02`, and independently `m13_strict`, where quarto ITSELF renders `## Oscar Strict`). Evidence: `scratchpad/s272/` (`posit-observation.txt`, `obs2/`, `axis/`, `empty/`, `unmask/`).
  - **(6) ✅ DECIDED (Session 274) — we FOLLOW `quarto render` and do NOT mirror Posit here. This is the ONE decision of the six that does not mirror Posit, and the reason is that Posit's own maintainers have disowned the behaviour upstream.** When a backtick fence's info string is refused by BOTH quarto's cell recognizer and pandoc's attribute parser, `quarto render` swallows the heading below it and **Posit's Outline shows that heading anyway** — Posit pairs the fence engine-blind and lists it as `(code cell)`. This model shows nothing, and that is now the recorded position. ✅ **Observed, not inferred** — `npm run observe:posit` (Session 273), `quarto.quarto 1.135.0`, `ours-loaded: false`, both controls green, every probe pre-rendered through real quarto and proved byte-identical to the harness's bytes. Three probes disagree: `r02_r5_qmd_markdown` (the R5 body as a `.qmd` under `engine: markdown`), `r03_digit_led_rmd` (`{2x, eval = FALSE}` — knitr claims it but pandoc refuses the rewritten class), `r05_hyphen_rmd` (`{r-foo, eval = FALSE}` — outside knitr's class). All three render `<h4>` count **0** and Posit lists the heading in all three.
  - **(7) ✅ DECIDED (Session 277) — we FOLLOW `quarto render` again: a fence that does not interrupt an open paragraph leaves the heading below it in the Outline, and Posit HIDES that heading. This is the SECOND of the seven decisions that does not mirror Posit, and it rests on the same rebuttal as (6).** Under pandoc's markdown reader only a column-0 BACKTICK fence interrupts an open paragraph; an opener indented 1-3, or a `~~~` opener, is paragraph text, so `quarto render` emits the heading below it. Posit's Outline is built from markdown-it, which is CommonMark, where such a fence DOES interrupt — so Posit shows only the tail heading. ✅ **Observed, not inferred** — `npm run observe:posit` (Session 277), operator go-ahead taken BEFORE the launch, `quarto.quarto 1.138.0`, `ours-loaded: false`, both controls green, 13 passing exit 0, every probe rendered through real quarto FIRST and proved byte-identical to the bytes the harness writes (`scratchpad/s277/posit-observation.txt`). The two DECIDING rows: `s01_para_bt_indent1` — quarto emits `Alpha Indent One` + `Bravo Tail`, Posit lists `Bravo Tail` alone; `s02_para_tilde_c0` — quarto emits `Charlie Tilde` + `Delta Tail`, Posit lists `Delta Tail` alone. ⚠ **The two CONTROLS are what make that a fence answer and not a blanket one:** `s03_para_bt_c0_ctrl` (a column-0 backtick, which pandoc DOES let interrupt) and `s04_gfm_bt_indent1` (the s01 bytes under `from: gfm`) — quarto hides the inner heading in both and so does Posit, so Posit opens column-0 fences exactly as quarto does and is READER-BLIND. Its answers on s01/s02 are about the indent and the fence character. The cost of mirroring Posit instead was measured before the decision: 255 of the 371 rows this rule corrects over 4,573 rendered probes are heading DELETIONS.
    - ⚠ **THE DECIDING EVIDENCE IS UPSTREAM AND PUBLIC, and it arrived after Session 273 filed this bullet.** [`quarto-dev/quarto#829`](https://github.com/quarto-dev/quarto/issues/829) — *"Outline includes headers in divs"*, **OPEN**, filed 2025-09-26 by **vezwork, the Quarto VS Code extension maintainer**, 3 comments, no fix landed. Its stated principle: *"it does seem that the outline should match the TOC that ends up in the rendered document."* `cscheid` (quarto-cli) replies that quarto-cli should be able to report the correct outline to clients and that this "seems like it should be a concern of quarto-cli"; vezwork answers *"yes please."* ⚠ **Its SHAPE is `:::` divs, not fences, so it is NOT a duplicate of our item** — what it settles is the PRINCIPLE, and the principle is the whole of this decision. Read firsthand (`gh issue view 829 --repo quarto-dev/quarto`), title/prose only, per `CLAUDE.md` §Clean-room boundary.
    - **Why that flips the default.** The clean-room rule's "the product mirrors Posit" rests on Posit being the *product* oracle — the thing users expect an Outline to do. Here Posit's own maintainers say the Outline should follow the render, so the engine-blind pairing is an acknowledged **open defect** on their side rather than a design choice. Mirroring it would copy a bug its authors intend to remove, and would have to be un-copied when they fix it.
    - **The cost of the road not taken, stated rather than hidden.** Mirroring Posit would be a WIDENING in the phantom-vs-`quarto render` direction and **its corpus reach was never measured** — no session has priced it. If a future session revisits this, that measurement is the first step, exactly as Session 271 priced R2 before deciding it.
    - ⚠ **NOTHING IS FILED ANYWHERE — measured at Session 274, not assumed.** `gh issue list --state all` on `rmsharp/vscode_quarto_ext` returns **zero** issues (issues ARE enabled; this project has never used them, `BACKLOG.md` is the tracker). Upstream: 29 searches on `quarto-dev/quarto-cli`, 38 on `quarto-dev/quarto` plus the archived `quarto-vscode`, complete label sweeps of `code-blocks` and `ast`, Discussions, and the open web — seven candidates, **all seven refuted** by an adversarial pass. ⚠ The negative is honest but bounded: GitHub issue search indexes code blocks poorly and nearly every quarto report states its shape inside a fenced reproducer. **Filing upstream remains OPEN and is NOT part of this decision** — this project has never filed upstream, and doing so would set a precedent the operator has not been asked for.
    - **Adjacent and worth citing if anyone does file:** [`quarto-dev/quarto-cli#14182`](https://github.com/quarto-dev/quarto-cli/issues/14182) — OPEN, filed by maintainer `mcanouil`, holding that *"attribute order should not matter. Both forms are valid Pandoc fenced code block attributes and neither represents an executable code cell."* Same decision point, opposite direction (a false POSITIVE), and the only place upstream states a governing principle for cell-vs-attributes.
    - ⚠ **The record was RE-MEASURED at Session 274, because a search agent contradicted it — and the agent was wrong.** It reported that the digit-led probe does not swallow and that the lever is the blank line before the heading. The exact harness bytes, extracted from the compiled `out/test/posit/runTest.js` rather than retyped, rendered through quarto 1.7.33: `r02` `<h4>`=0, `r03`=0, `r05`=0, `r01`=1, `r06`=1 — **Session 273's figures exactly**. A 2×2 on `r03` shows the lever is the **TRAILING BARE FENCE**, not the blank line: with it `<h4>`=0 under both blank-line spellings, without it `<h4>`=1 under both. The raw HTML puts `#### Charlie After Chunk` **inside `<pre><code>`**, and emits `{.2x .cell-code}` — knitr DID claim the chunk and pandoc DID refuse the class, which is the S273 mechanism read directly rather than inferred. The agent's probe had no trailing fence: **the exact error Session 273 filed as Learning #573**.
    - Pinned by three rows in `test/unit/qmd-model.test.ts`'s R5 suite and one in `test/integration/suite/outline.test.ts`, each now naming this decision instead of reading as provisional. Transcript: `scratchpad/s273/posit-observation.txt`.

  - ⚠ **A black-box confirmation is now CHEAP for (1) and (2) as well** — `npm run observe:posit` exists and takes probes; both still rest on a bundle grep and neither has been observed. That is a session of its own, and nothing yet depends on it.
  - ⚠ Delete this bullet only when something makes all **seven** answers unnecessary — a decision record is not open work, and it is filed here because `BACKLOG.md` is where a session looks for "has this been settled?". The same decisions are in `CHANGELOG.md` under Sessions 267, 270, 271, 272, 274 and 277.

- [ ] **RECORD — the div classes measured NOT to consume a heading, so a future session does not re-measure them** (Session 257, while bounding the callout rule; **MEASURED** — rendered, and the `<hN>` tag confirmed in the RAW HTML rather than through the extractor, since `.column-margin` and `.aside` were the two at risk of a false positive from the extractor's chrome-skipping; `scratchpad/s257/probes/p28`). `.column-margin`, `.aside`, `.hidden`, `.cell`, `.card`, `.panel-sidebar`, `.panel-input`, `.column-body`, `.columns`, `.unnumbered`, a cross-reference div `::: {#fig-…}` and — importantly — **bare `.tabset`, which is NOT a tabset; only `.panel-tabset` is** — each keeps a real heading, and this model is already correct on every one. ⚠ Not a defect: this is the NEGATIVE result that bounds the `:::` div family, filed because its absence is what makes the family look open-ended. Delete this bullet once the `.panel-tabset` and format-gated items above are closed. ⚠ **Session 290 RE-MEASURED THE WHOLE SPACE AND IT IS CLOSED:** of 90 candidate classes, rendered first AND second against a callout and a tabset (687 probes, `scratchpad/s290/registry/class_table.tsv`), only the nine NAMES quarto registers a block handler for ever change a dispatch — `callout`, `callout-note/-tip/-warning/-important/-caution`, `panel-tabset`, and `content-visible` / `content-hidden` — and the first of them in source order wins.

- [ ] **⚠ LETTER/ROMAN list markers are accepted by only THREE of the nine readers, and this model accepts them under all nine** (found by Session 249 while measuring the decimal reader split; **MEASURED** — rendered, `scratchpad/s249/r3`, 72 documents, all exit 0; **PRE-EXISTING in kind and widened by Session 249**; BOTH directions; MEDIUM-HIGH). A letter or roman marker (`a.`, `clxxxviii.`, `cclxxxviii.`) opens a list only under **no `from:` at all, `markdown`, and `commonmark_x`**; `markdown_strict`, `markdown_mmd`, `markdown_phpextra`, `markdown_github`, `gfm` and `commonmark` open NOTHING for it at any length. That is pandoc's `fancy_lists` extension axis and it **crosses `commonmarkDialect` in both directions**, so the existing flag cannot express it — `commonmark_x` is inside the gate and accepts them; `markdown_strict` is outside it and does not. ⚠ **BOTH directions are live:** 8 phantoms under the four `markdown_*` readers at roman 10+ (the measured cost of Session 249, `r3/mstrict_r10` and its family) plus the pre-existing ones at `a.`/9-character romans, AND 2 DELETED headings under `commonmark_x`, where quarto renders `h2:bravo` and this model reports nothing (`r3/cmx_a01`, `cmx_r09`). ⚠ Size it as a NEW reader flag beside `commonmarkDialect`, not a widening of it. Pinned as Session 249 R3.

- [ ] **⚠ An ordered marker whose list is ALREADY OPEN above strips at EVERY underline column, and this build keeps it at an outer one** (the one disclosed gap in Session 247's rule; **MEASURED** — rendered, `scratchpad/s247/r2/a_second_same`, `a_second_blank`, `a_third`, `a_same_num`, `f_ol_open_ol`, `c_inner_sib_u0`, `c_inner_sib_u4`, seven witnesses; TEXT divergence only, never a heading gained or lost; corpus weight **ZERO** over 60,998 documents; MEDIUM). `1.  alpha` / `2.  bravo` / `---` renders `ol>h2` and `h2:bravo`, and this model reports `h2:2.  bravo`. Where a list is already open its own parser claims a sibling marker before the header parser sees it, so the setext parse never gets the raw line. ⚠ **Answering it needs the KIND and DELIMITER of the open list, which is why it is a container-state change rather than a one-line widening:** `-   alpha` / `1.  bravo` / `---` and `1.  alpha` / `1)  bravo` / `---` both start a NEW list and both KEEP the marker (`r2/a_kind_ul_ol`, `a_kind_dot_par`), and the `ColumnStack` (`columnKinds` until Session 331) carries only `list`/`definition`. ⚠ Session 247's own G2 guards those two rows, so a fix must keep them green. ⚠ It is also the term that would let the LAZY-QUOTE decline become a narrower column rule instead: `>` / `1.  bravo` / `    ---` and `> 1.  alpha` / `1.  bravo` / `    ---` reach the strip with IDENTICAL scanner state and quarto answers them oppositely (`scratchpad/s247/dbg`). Pinned as Session 247 R1.

- [ ] **⚠ An INDENTED fenced code block inside a list item is not recognised, so its contents are parsed as markdown** (found by Session 246 while pricing its own change; **MEASURED** — rendered, `scratchpad/s246/r11/h_blank_atx` against its top-level twin `h_top_blank`; **PRE-EXISTING** — the Session 243 build makes the identical error on the identical bytes; phantom direction; MEDIUM). `-   item one` / `    ``` ` / (blank) / `    # ATX Inside` / `    ``` ` renders NOTHING — the pair is a fenced code block — and this model reports `h1:ATX Inside`. ⚠ **The probe uses an ATX ON PURPOSE**, because an ATX needs no body run at all and so is independent of Session 246's change; the top-level twin is correct in both builds, which localises the defect to the INDENT. ⚠ **The model reaches the right answer on several neighbouring shapes BY ACCIDENT** rather than by recognising the fence — `f_setext` and `f_atx` in `scratchpad/s246/r10` are clean only because the body run or `paragraphOpen` happened to decline there. ⚠ It is the ONLY cost of Session 246 in a 60,998-document corpus sweep: 2 rendered rows, one document duplicated (`scratchpad/s201/adv/reg/reg_12.qmd`), against 585 recovered. Pinned as Session 246 R1.

- [ ] **A list marker that shares its parent's LINE never opens the inner content column** (found by an independent agent during Session 246 and verified firsthand; **MEASURED** — rendered, `scratchpad/s246/r8/n2_i4`, `n2_i4d`, `n4_i8`; **PRE-EXISTING and unmoved** by Session 246; LOW). `- - b` / `    -` renders `ul>ul>h2` with the text `b` and no build reports it: the inner item's content column is 4 and this model pushes 5. The wide spelling `-   -   b` / `        ---` needs column 8 and the model never pushes it at all. ⚠ **Every OTHER indent in both sweeps is right** (`r8/n2_i0`, `n2_i2`, `n4_i0`, `n4_i4`), which is what makes this a column-arithmetic defect in `listItemContentColumn` rather than a container defect. Pinned as Session 246 R3.

- [ ] **⚠ Six terms of the marked-blank rule are UNOBSERVABLE over 746,496 enumerated documents, and two of them may now be dead** (found by Session 245's mutation hunt, 28 mutants of which 22 died; **MEASURED** — `scratchpad/s245/hunt.json`, alphabets built from what each surviving term reads; LOW). The survivors are: the pre-pass's `!open` term and its underline-line SKIP; the `i + 1 < lines.length` bounds check; `quoteOpensHere`'s `!inQuote` term; and BOTH `pendingFreshBlock` assignments. ⚠ **`pendingFreshBlock` is the one worth looking at.** Session 244 added it with a long justification — the counter has to be 1 HERE and 1 AGAIN on the line below, or `>` / `> title` / `---` loses its heading — but under Session 245's narrowing that document no longer enters the branch at all (the line below the marker is not an underline), so the deferral may be genuinely dead and its comment misleading. ⚠ **Do NOT simply delete them:** 0 disagreements on one alphabet is evidence of unobservability, not proof of deadness, and this is refactoring, which needs plan-mode approval. Size it as a measurement pass that either kills each term with a rendered row or removes it with the enumeration as evidence.

- [ ] **An ordered marker EATEN by its own setext underline leaves the list's content column on `contentColumns`, so a quote below it is read one container in when quarto reads it at top level** (found by Session 242's 3,200,000-document pre/post enumeration, NOT by its 49,348-document corpus sweep and NOT by its 1,613 rendered probes, both of which scored 0 broken; **MEASURED** — rendered, `scratchpad/s242/r5/cls03`; **PRE-EXISTING and newly REACHABLE**, not newly created; **phantom direction**; corpus weight measured at **ZERO**; LOW). `1. item` / `---` renders `h2:1. item` — the underline claims the RAW line, the marker survives into the heading TEXT, and NO list opens — but this model still pushes content column 3. So `1. item` / `---` / `   > quoted one` / ` ---` / `- item` renders `h2:1. item` alone and this model adds a phantom `h2:quoted one`. ⚠ **THE BULLET TWIN IS A CORRECT RECOVERY, WHICH IS WHAT LOCALISES IT** — `- item` / `---` renders `h2:item` with the marker STRIPPED, so a list really is open there and quarto renders both headings (`r5/cls04`, and `cls02` for the `- body` spelling). The divergence is about whether the marker was CONSUMED, not about the body-row floor Session 242 shipped. ⚠ Size it against the container-maintenance rows: the fix is to suppress the column push where the setext parse has already claimed the marker line, which is the same ordering question Session 241 met at the quote-entry stash (Learning #446). Pinned in both directions at `test/unit/qmd-model.test.ts` Session 242 R2.

- [ ] **Heading TEXT keeps runs of internal spaces that quarto collapses to one** (found by Session 241 in the same classification pass; **MEASURED** — rendered, `scratchpad/s241/r4/ws15`; TEXT divergence only; LOW). `:   def item` used as a setext title renders `h2:: def item` and this model reports `h2::   def item`. ⚠ **Orthogonal to any column rule and PRE-EXISTING as a transform** — it is newly VISIBLE only because Session 241 forms the heading at all, and a heading-COUNT scorer reports the row as agreement (Session 231's gotcha 3). ⚠ The fix is a whitespace collapse in `setextTitleText`, but check it against the ATX path and the marker-stripping paths first, which share the same text pipeline. Pinned as Session 241 R3.

- [ ] **⚠ `scratchpad/` IS UNTRACKED BUT NOT IGNORED, so a single `git add -A` stages ~85,000 files and ~2.4 GB into a commit** (found by Session 240 the hard way — its own close-out commit did exactly this and had to be `git reset --soft`-ed and rebuilt; **MEASURED** — the reverted commit contained **85,636** `scratchpad/` paths against 42 intended ones; repo-hygiene direction; **LOW effort, HIGH blast radius**). `.gitignore` has no `scratchpad/` rule; 240 sessions have kept the directory out of history only by never running `git add -A` at the repo root, which is a convention rather than a guard. ⚠ **The failure is silent in exactly the way this project has already been bitten by twice** — `check-package` still passed (it reads `vsce ls`, which obeys `.vscodeignore`, not `.gitignore`), `npm test` still passed, and nothing in the build, test or packaging pipeline can observe it; only reading `git show --name-only | wc -l` can. That is the same blind spot recorded for the Session 174 `scratchpad/` `.vsix` packaging leak. ⚠ **The fix is one line** (`scratchpad/` in `.gitignore`) and it is deliberately NOT taken here, because it changes repo-wide behaviour outside this session's deliverable and the operator may have left the directory merely untracked on purpose — e.g. so that `git status` keeps surfacing it. ⚠ If it is adopted, check nothing currently depends on `scratchpad/` being listed by `git status --short` (Session 240's own Phase 0 report reads it there), and consider whether a `check-package`-style deny-by-default guard on commit size is the more general answer.

Pinned AT the phantom, deliberately, as Session 240 C6 — it is what stops `!lazyQuotedUnderline` being dropped from the quote-end block. ⚠ **SESSION 241 WIDENED ITS REACH AND SAYS SO HERE RATHER THAN LEAVING IT TO BE FOUND.** The old floor blocked a whole class of these rows BY ACCIDENT — the second rule row sits at the SAME column as the title it underlines, and `indentColumn(line) > indentColumn(title)` is false there — so replacing that floor with the container-stack membership test opens them. Measured, it flips the answer in both directions and position matters: a child holding `{julia}` above an `{r}` cell renders **exit 0** where the include-free control renders exit 1; a child holding `{r}` above a `{python}` cell renders **exit 1** where the control renders exit 0; the same include placed AFTER the `{r}` cell leaves it exit 1; and it recurses (doc → child → grandchild). S165 declines (`undefined` → the per-cell approximation), which cannot create a false positive but does not remove the pre-existing one: replayed end-to-end, the two include rows stay cardinal FPs, byte-identical to the pre-S165 tree. **The alternative was measured too:** answering `"ambiguous"` on any include-bearing document removes both, and costs the knitr-only fields on every OTHER include-bearing document — the ordinary book chapter, where quarto really is knitr (one corpus row shows exactly that). A rare conditional FP against a common lost TP is the reverse of the trade this project's doctrine settles (S162's 1-against-46, S163's 26-against-144), which is why the decline was chosen and the decision filed rather than made silently. A real fix reads the included files — off-editor `fs.readFile`, re-trigger on the child's edits — which is the same architectural gap the `_quarto.yml`-from-a-nested-`.qmd` item describes. **Do them together.**

Quarto's `kRmdExtensions` is `[".rmd", ".rmarkdown"]` and `isRMarkdownFileName` matches both, so `documentEngineForScoping` answers `"knitr"` for a `doc.Rmarkdown` and the oracle row `.Rmarkdown {python} + cache` agrees with a real `quarto render` (exit 1). But `package.json`'s `contributes.languages` registers only `.qmd`/`.rmd`/`.Rmd` for the `quarto` languageId, and `activationEvents` carries `workspaceContains:**/*.{qmd,rmd,Rmd}` — so opening a `.Rmarkdown` gives no diagnostics, no completion and no highlighting unless the user selects the language by hand. **Not a defect S170 introduced** (the extension has never opened those files) and the fix is a one-line `package.json` addition, but it should be grounded first: confirm the grammar and every feature behave on a real `.Rmarkdown`, since nothing has ever exercised that path. The condition is that the render actually invokes **knitr** — with no code chunk in `s.qmd`, knitr never runs and `s.rmarkdown` survives, which is what a first, under-specified probe here measured and wrongly recorded as a non-reproduction (#567). Scoped by control: a same-stem `s.md` **survives**, and `other.rmarkdown` survives, so the collateral is specific to the `.rmarkdown` extension and the same stem. This form is the dangerous one — exit 0, correct-looking output, and an unrelated file gone. ⚠ Not this extension's bug, and it reaches no corpus document (the 3,383-document corpus is 2,051 `.rmd` + 1,332 `.qmd`, zero `.rmarkdown`, measured), but any future work here — or any tooling that renders user documents — must copy first.

A macro line is a block only when whitespace, its own argument groups, or further NON-INLINE macros follow it: `\clearpage and more prose`, `\clearpage.`, `\clearpage % a comment` and `\maketitle \textbf{b}` are all prose to pandoc and blocks to us. Carrying it into the narrowing was measured deleting `\vspace*{1em}` (the star is part of the starred form), `\vspace2` (TeX's single-token argument, i.e. `\vspace{2}`) and `\newcommand{\foo}{bar}` — three of the eleven spellings the item above named. **A fix needs an arity-tolerant tail rule**, not the strict one; see `PROJECT_LEARNINGS.md` #258.

Pandoc's raw-LaTeX block takes the following line's leading `[...]` as `\maketitle`'s optional argument, so quarto's setext TITLE loses the bracket group while ours keeps it. It is a TEXT divergence, not a fabricated heading — both renderers emit an `<h1>` here. **This is the ONE of Session 191's ten new phantoms that the trigger-removal control does NOT explain**, and it is pinned in the unit suite with the `<div>` control that shows quarto's own text changing to ours once the macro is gone. A fix needs the scanner to model a macro's argument spanning two source lines.

A document whose front matter closes with `...` instead of `---` does not render, so its truth row is empty and the scorer reads our headings as phantoms against a document that has no heading truth at all. **This is the already-filed `FRONTMATTER_CLOSE` item wearing a different hat** (see "`FRONTMATTER_CLOSE` accepts `...` as a front-matter terminator and quarto REFUSES TO RENDER such a document" below) — the new part is the *harness* consequence. **The cheap fix belongs in the scorer, not the model:** `render190.sh` already records the exit code in column 2, so any future score should EXCLUDE non-zero-exit rows and report their count separately rather than treating them as agreement data.

- [ ] **A BLANK LINE INSIDE AN INDENTED CODE BLOCK re-arms the following code line as a setext title** (found by Session 193's own completeness pass over the gaps its eight blind lenses left; **MEASURED**; **PRE-EXISTING** — identical on the pre-S193 build; phantom direction; LOW by volume, and the mechanism is worth more than the count). `Intro.` / (blank) / `    zzz` / (blank) / `    India Run Title` / `===` renders NO heading — a blank line does not end an indented code block for pandoc, so all of it is one code block — and we emit `h1:India Run Title`. The cause is that the blank resets `consecutiveBody` to 0 and the next code line increments it straight back to the eligible 1, so the code-RUN exception never gets to speak. **PROVEN BY CONTROL** in both directions: delete the blank and the run exception fires and agrees with quarto; and a LONE indented line really IS a setext title, so the fix is *not* "an indented line can never be a title". - [ ] **`A. x` — a single capital and EXACTLY one space — is not a list, and we open a container for it anyway** (created knowingly by Session 189 as the FP-safe side of a measured trade; **MEASURED** — the sole residual phantom over the 2,394-document marker corpus; LOW). Measured: a tag line opens a block only if, after the tag name, the line ENDS AT a `>` (trailing whitespace allowed) or contains no `>` at all. `<div>`, `<div>x</div>` and `<title>Doc</title id="y">` are block; `<div> trailing text` and `</note> and that is deliberate.` are ordinary paragraph text and we fabricate a heading below both. Both need state a per-line predicate does not have. **16 residual phantoms across the 2,223-document corpus, in four classes, each pinned in a test.**

- [ ] **DOCUMENTATION DEFECT: `SETEXT_H1`'s docstring claims we match quarto by declining the ATX-swallow, and the claim is refuted** (found by Session 182; **MEASURED**; **PRE-EXISTING**; LOW, but it is load-bearing prose that a future session will reason from). Measured on the real render path, quarto **does** swallow: `# Heading Above` / `===` renders `<h1># Heading Above</h1>` — literal `#` and all — and `# Heading Above` / `-` renders `<h2># Heading Above</h2>`. Our declining it is a deliberate, defensible choice (an outline row reading `# Heading Above` would be noise), but it is a DIVERGENCE, not a match, and it costs 9 residual rows where our heading text differs from quarto's. ⚠ **SESSION 230 MEASURED THAT THE DECLINE IS NO LONGER BLANKET — the swallow IS implemented at top level today and the residual is QUOTE-SCOPED.** Probed, `# Adv Quote Head` / `===` now reports the literal `# Adv Quote Head`, which is exactly what quarto renders; the same shape INSIDE a block quote reports `Adv Quote Head` and quarto renders `# Adv Quote Head`. Two rendered witnesses, `scratchpad/s225/all/07852b580fc7.qmd` (`s225/f/f04`) and `5a3b2a9f61db.qmd` (`s214/adv/x5_quote`). So a session acting on this item should read it as *the swallow does not fire inside a quote*, not as *we decline the swallow*. Session 182 depends on the swallow being real — `prevWasAtxHeading` exists precisely to recover the block closure it causes — so the docstring now contradicts working code twenty lines away. ⚠ **EXTENDED BY SESSION 199 WITH A CONTAINER SPELLING, and that session's ONE introduced error is this item.** The swallow was only ever measured at column 0 — `SETEXT_UNDERLINE_RUN`'s docstring said in as many words that "the swallow was never measured inside a container, so nothing here is claimed about one". It IS claimed now: inside `-   item one`, the document `    # Container Heading Above` / `    ===` renders `<h1># Container Heading Above</h1>`, literal `#` and all (rendered, `scratchpad/s199/comp/sw_after_c4.qmd`). Before S199 this model AGREED there BY ACCIDENT — ` {0,3}` could not see the heading at column 4, so the line fell through and the `===` claimed it as a setext title with the `#` intact; now the ATX row consumes it first and strips the `#`, exactly as it always has at column 0. **The behaviour is therefore now UNIFORM where it used to disagree with itself across columns, and no heading is deleted or fabricated in either spelling** — both renderers emit one `h1` and only the TEXT differs. Closing it means reversing S182's deliberate product choice (an outline row reading `# Heading Above` is noise), which is why S199 disclosed rather than closed it. Pinned with both spellings and the indent-1 row S199 RECOVERED (quarto's own text, exactly) as the last test of the Session 199 describe in `test/unit/qmd-model.test.ts`.

The FP GUARD pin stayed green throughout, which is the evidence that the two surfaces stayed separate.

- [ ] **A `---` on the document's LAST line: our line split and quarto's disagree about whether the HR exemption applies** (found by Session 177 while pinning its own scanner's boundaries; **REASONED, NOT MEASURED — treat as unverified**; expected INERT). Quarto's `isYamlDelimiter` declines a `---` only when `index > 0 && index < srcLines.length - 1` with both neighbours blank. Our `quartoYamlRegions` splits on `/\r?\n/`, so a document ending in a newline yields a trailing EMPTY line that quarto's `rangedLines` does not produce — which can make our `index < length - 1` guard true where quarto's is false, i.e. we read a trailing `para`/blank/`---`/EOF as a horizontal rule where quarto opens a region. Argued inert because a region opening on the last line swallows no cells, so no flag can differ; NOT confirmed by measurement, and the argument is exactly the kind that has been wrong twice in this project. Cheap to settle: one `quarto render` of that shape plus a `quartoYamlRegions` call on the same bytes.

The COLUMN-0 sequence half of this item is likewise now a decline (`readTruthiness` returns `"unreadable"` for an empty token with no children) rather than a silent non-selection.

- [ ] **Node-property spellings of the override, and the truthy values L6/L7 deliberately gave up** (Session 164, all verified firsthand; **MIS-LABELLED until Session 165 — see below**). We compare a RAW token where quarto resolves the PARSED node, so each of these declines and falls back to the language approximation: `engine: &a markdown` / `!!str markdown` / `*alias`; a TRUTHY plain-scalar body (`knitr:` / `  true` renders **exit 1** — knitr really is selected); a truthy node-property value (`knitr: !!bool true`, exit 1); and a NON-EMPTY block scalar (`jupyter: |` + a body). Declining is what killed three introduced cardinal FPs, so any fix must resolve the node properly — and inherits S163's `!!str false` / `!!bool "false"` trap plus a document-level anchor table. **S165 UPDATE — the old `FP-SAFE direction` label was wrong, and the cost is larger than filed.** Declining is FP-safe only in the sense that it adds no NEW flag; on any document holding an `{r}` cell the caller then reaches knitr through the per-cell approximation anyway, so each of these spellings leaves a live cardinal-sin FALSE POSITIVE on that cell — the very one the override exists to remove (the S165 §9 completeness critic caught the mislabelling, and the Phase 0 ranking would have under-ranked these items because of it). Because a decline no longer means "use the cell language" but "do not answer at all", S165 had to classify every unreadable spelling explicitly (`readEngineScalar`, `readTruthiness`), and the §9 review found that shipping them as UNMATCHED produced twelve cardinal false positives (all fixed, all pinned). The set that now declines, each measured firsthand at S165: `engine: &a markdown` / `*alias` / `!!str markdown` / `>-`+folded body / an empty token with an indented continuation; the nested `execute:`/`  engine: &a …`; `execute: *a` and `execute: &a`; an engine-named key with `!!bool true`, `&a true`, a `|` body, or a COLUMN-0 sequence body; and an engine-named key that is bare (indistinguishable from that column-0 body). Two of these are measured LOST TRUE POSITIVES rather than merely missed narrowings — `engine: &a knitr` and `engine: |` + a literal `markdown` body both render **exit 1** (quarto really does end up on knitr) and we are now silent. Separating them from their exit-0 twins needs real YAML node resolution including chomping semantics (`>-` strips the newline and matches, `|` keeps it and does not), which is exactly the trap S163 filed.

S164 declines (`"ambiguous"` → agnostic scope) whenever the front matter selects more than one engine, so it is FP-safe; the cost is the knitr-only fields on such documents. **The §9 review established the bail is over-broad for one pair**: for `engine:` vs `execute.engine:` the winner is decided entirely by document order (both write the same `format.execute.engine` slot) and the project list provably does NOT change it — measured with and without the reorder. Document order IS visible to us, so that pair could be resolved exactly; any order-aware fix must also let an unrecognized LATER `engine:` clear the selection, or it reintroduces the FP L7 closed.

- [ ] **`validate-yaml: false` does NOT suppress the 26 top-level keys PANDOC validates, and S163's document gate silences all of them** (found by the Session 163 §9 review as a false universal in S163's own docstrings; the exception set **measured firsthand by S163** with an exhaustive sweep; **CAUSED BY S163**, knowingly, as the FP-safe side of a measured trade). S163's comments claimed the front-matter **format NAME** was "THE ONE SURFACE" the flag does not suppress. It is not. Swept all **170** top-level front-matter keys this feature can actually flag — the domain that matters is `isWrongValue("banana", field) === true`, not "keys quarto rejects", because a key we cannot flag can never cost a true positive (the S162 `layout` lesson) — one `quarto render --no-execute` each with `validate-yaml: false` set: **26 still render exit 1**, and each of the 26 renders exit 1 without the flag too, so every one is a real value error we reported before S163 and are silent on now. The survivors: `ascii`, `cite-method`, `citeproc`, `columns`, `dpi`, `email-obfuscation`, `epub-chapter-level`, `eol`, `fail-if-warnings`, `html-q-tags`, `incremental`, `ipynb-output`, `listings`, `number-offset`, `preserve-tabs`, `reference-location`, `section-divs`, `shift-heading-level-by`, `slide-level`, `strip-comments`, `tab-stop`, `toc`, `toc-depth`, `top-level-division`, `trace`, `wrap`. **Mechanism:** they are consumed by **pandoc**, whose Aeson decoder rejects them (`Error in $: expected Bool, but encountered String`, `Unknown wrap method "banana"`, constructor-tag errors for `eol`/`ipynb-output`), rather than by the quarto YAML-schema layer `validate-yaml` gates; `number-offset` is quarto's own non-schema check (`Invalid value for number-offset`). The other **144** render exit 0, so suppression is correct for them and the design stands — 144 unconditional false positives removed against 26 lost true positives, and an over-flag is the cardinal sin. **Why it was not fixed in S163:** the survivor set is a property of *pandoc's* option decoder, so it varies with pandoc version AND with output format (the sweep used the default html format; several survivors are revealjs/beamer keys that happen to be decoded anyway), which makes hard-coding these 26 names exactly the brittle transcription Learning #174 warns about. A faithful fix needs a principled source for "keys pandoc validates independently of quarto's schema" — the sweep harness is the starting point, not the answer. MEDIUM (26 lost TPs on common keys including `toc`).

- [ ] **Four YAML spellings of `validate-yaml: false` disarm quarto and we keep squiggling** (found by the Session 163 §9 review, **all re-measured firsthand by S163** against controls identical but for `false`→`true`; **PRE-EXISTING** — the pre-S163 tree flagged these documents too, so S163 simply does not reach them). `isValidationDisabledValue` (`src/core/validate-yaml.ts`) compares the RAW token, while quarto compares the PARSED value (`annotation.result["validate-yaml"] !== false`), so these all render **exit 0** while we still flag — the cardinal sin: (a) **anchored** `validate-yaml: &a false`; (b) **tagged** `validate-yaml: !!bool false`; (c) **aliased** `xx: &a false` + `validate-yaml: *a`; (d) **value on the next line** `validate-yaml:` / `  false` (the front-matter enumerator emits no `rawToken` for a key whose scalar is on a following line). **⚠ Do NOT fix this by stripping the node property and re-comparing** — measured, `!!str false` renders **exit 1** (it is the string, so validation stays ON) while `!!bool "false"` renders **exit 0** (the tag coerces the quoted scalar), so an explicit tag INVERTS the quoted-scalar rule that the rest of the predicate depends on. A correct fix resolves the YAML node rather than pattern-matching it, and (c) additionally needs a document-level anchor table. Note the project already has a node-property precedent in the FN-safe direction (`src/core/yaml-value-check.ts`'s `/^[[\]{}|>&*!]/` skip, S125) — but that one SKIPS such tokens, which here would be the wrong direction, since skipping means "do not disarm" and so preserves the FP. LOW-MEDIUM (rare shapes, but each is a live cardinal-sin FP, and the same predicate is the one BACKLOG names for reuse when `_quarto.yml` is widened).

For an `{r}` cell whose partitioned option text is guessed `"knitr"` — the lens reports the heuristic as at least one line with no colon and no leading whitespace, with every non-blank line either ending in `,` or containing `=` — quarto returns `yaml: undefined` and validates nothing in that cell, so any value renders exit 0 while `findCellOptionLines` still emits and we still flag. - [ ] **Session 332 residuals of the paragraph-title scan (the S318 item's part (9))** (filed by Session 332; MEASURED by the second review round's performance and mutation lenses, `scratchpad/s332/lens2-*`, untracked). (a) A question costs (distinct indents kept) x (open columns): a 7.2 MB document built from a stair of 400 nested tab-indented containers, 800 distinct code-depth matching indents and 16,000 underline-shaped lines takes 8.0 s, 4.6 s of it in `interruptedAfterFirst` (x3.3, x4.2, x5.4 per doubling), about 0.3 s per MB^1.5, and never slower than the parent (1,714 ms on the parent and 369 ms here at n=100); a question passes a few microseconds per indent only above about 3,000 open columns, a 4.5 MB stair. Cure idea (not adopted): keep the last answer for (the columns array identity, the number of kept indents) as an OPTIMISATION only, because correctness must not depend on the identity (the first version did, and restarted); the lens's scratch patch took n=400 from 8.0 s to 3.3 s, equal to its control. (b) Constant factors: 1.20x on a text of lines that begin with a long `=` run (the hoisted `setextUnderlineLevel` now runs on every line under these readers and its failing tail backtracks over the run; 4 MB: 167 against 138 ms), 1.04 to 1.06x on long whitespace-led lines behind an early interrupter (the scan reads every line once where the parent's `.some` stopped). Cure idea: compute `commonmarkUnderlineLevel` only when the line's first non-blank character is `=` or `-`. (c) `htmlBlockMayBeOpenAbove`'s capped 200-line back-walk is 27% of the time of a 160,000-line paragraph (linear and pre-existing, about 1.8 us a line); keep the index of the last blank line and of the last `<` line instead. (d) Test edits no row notices, low value: a pinned count or alphabet entry of the oracle file, the comparator's default candidate, and the warm rows not checking that ONE scan is kept (asking a new scan each time passes them). `ParagraphInterruptScan` is the only exported class in the 15,000-line `model.ts` while `ColumnStack` has its own file (placement, optional).

- [ ] **`scanMathRegions` searches for a closer past the end of the opener's line, which costs 1.08x on real documents and up to 1.4x on a text whose openers have no closer on their own line** (filed by Session 327; MEASURED by the review round's performance lens, `scratchpad/s327/lens-performance/`, untracked). `nextInlineCloser` reads to the first closer-shaped `$` or the end of the run, and the use site throws away a closer on a later line, so an opener with no closer on its own line pays one extra pass and never more (the searches are disjoint). Median of 9 alternating runs, the scan only, parent -> cure, load 4.3: `$a\n` + 10,000,000 `x` + `\n$$ y` 75.7 -> 108.2 ms (1.43x); 200,000 lines of `$a` / `b$` 20.9 -> 25.9 ms (1.24x); 1,067 real `.qmd` and `.md` files (22.1 million characters, 5,770 `$`) 117.9 -> 127.5 ms (1.08x; a second run 1.28x); pairs, `$5 and $10` prose and lines that refuse every opener 0.99 to 1.06x. Through `findMathRegions` the real corpus shows none (0.98 to 0.995) and the 20-million-character shape 1.16 to 1.20x (+47 ms). The lens's cure, about 14 lines, proved against the parent on 900,000 strings and passing both test files in a private copy: `nextInlineCloser` stops at a `\n` and returns its negated position, and the scan keeps the line that holds no closer so a later opener on it does not search again. ⚠ Do NOT find the end of the line with `s.indexOf("\n", p + 1)` per search: it is quadratic on a long line with many matches (`$a$ ` x 1,000,000 took 45 s against 92 ms). Session 327 did not take it: the cost is a constant on shapes the parent already read in linear time, and the cure had just been proved by the oracle and 94 mutants. Not worked on.

Nanoseconds a call, parent -> new (`headingContentRefused`, 200,000 calls, median of 7 interleaved runs, before the two Maps were made lazy): plain 204 -> 246 (198 after), a code span 183 -> 442, `[g](#g)` 268 -> 1,197, `<span class=x>raw</span>` 410 -> 820, macros 688 -> 1,599, a mix of link, code, tag, macro and math 718 -> 3,202. The cure the lens tried: answer the FIRST question per key by reading (the old loops, which would then live in two places) and build the table at the SECOND: link 1,154 -> 329 ns, mixed 3,482 -> 1,832, the four `endsHtmlBlock` rows 0.97 to 1.05 of the parent, linear still at 5M and 20M characters; its copy passed the oracle file 42 of 42 and the clock rows 21 of 21 (`scratchpad/s325/design-v7.patch`, untracked, made against `0e5aeab`; `indexOfMarker` has changed since). **Not done** because the cost is microseconds and the second spelling is code to keep in step with the first: do it if a real document shows the cost, or when `endsHtmlBlock` is next touched. None of it is needed for the linear bound or the answer. Short lines whose first `{` fails and another follows: `a {{ b` 40 -> 77 ns, `{ { { x` 56 -> 109 ns (the `Int32Array`); lines that never reach the table are the same or faster (the first `{` is found with `indexOf`: `none` 359 -> 13 ns, `x {a} y` 42 -> 43, an image with an id block 77 -> 50), and the readers show no difference at 200,000 such lines (the scan, about 550 ms, dominates). Four spellings were measured and proved against the parent (1,000,000+ strings, 0 differences): (a) a `}` guard in the failure branch, `if (lineText.indexOf("}", next) === -1) break;`, which skips the allocation when nothing later can close and makes those lines 2 to 2.3 times faster than the cure (two stray `{`: 610 -> 263 ns); (b) building the table at the SECOND failed read; (c) `buildTagCloses` (Session 325) generalised to `buildQuoteCloses(text, close)`, which deletes `braceClosers` and the `base` bookkeeping (36 lines out, 9 in, at most 9% slower on one shape) but couples the pandoc attribute-quote grammar, measured by rendering in Session 222, to the HTML-tag quote grammar; (d) `charCodeAt` in both loops (9 to 14%). The table holds one `Int32Array` entry a CHARACTER, not a brace (an index by the ordinal of the `{`, or a `Map`, would hold only the braces; resident size stays small because the untouched pages are never written: 135 MB peak for 20,000,000 `{`, 2.4 GB for 500,000,000). Naming deviations from the neighbours (`buildTagCloses`, `buildGroupEnds`), none functional: `from` means the column of the `{` in `braceClosers` and the column after it in `braceCloser`, the table is `closers` where they have `closes`, its non-`{` entries are 0 where `buildGroupEnds` fills -1, and the array-destructuring swap appears nowhere else in `src`. A change here reopens code the 47 + 25 mutants and the oracle just verified, for tens of nanoseconds: do it only if a profile of a real document ever shows `braceGroups`.

(a) **Quarto's `=` fix-up for `:::` openers** — `lpegfenceddiv.attempt_to_fix_fenced_div`'s precondition: ASCII-literal `[A-Za-z_#.=][A-Za-z0-9.=_-]*` or quoted tokens, `}` immediately before end of line, only the FIRST bad `=`, even parity on all four quote counts, a rescan, TABs expanded first — and the **`{{<`-in-a-double-quoted-value refusal**. The plan routes both to the legacy reading, which also keeps HEAD's deletions on **9 fix-up-BLOCKED refused shapes** (fill-0 `b16`, `b19`, `b20`, `b28`, `b29`, `T4`, `Q1`, `Q3`, `Q4` — e.g. `::: {.callout-note data-x= y}` plus trailing spaces: quarto renders `Inside`, the model hides it); `{.callout-note = x}` glues into the fallback class `{.callout-note=x}`; and the fix-up also fires on HEADING lines (fill-0 `P7`), so the model's heading text differs. (d) **The commonmark_x attribute grammar**: 58 of 125 div verdicts differ from markdown; under `from: commonmark_x` 64 of 124 callout variants hide a heading quarto renders — still true after the plan's P4; fill-1's `_cmx` DELETE rows; `title=''` and `title="   "`; the standalone `{…}` attribute line; the fence label `i01`; the 3-space-indented opener phantom `T29c`; and the questions of *"Source 3 now carries the reader split, but Sources 2 and 3's OTHER dialect questions were never asked"*. So the nested `title="x` tabset deletion (quarto `['h2:After Inner']`, the model `[]`), the glued phantom and `W1` stay, as do the multi-line accepted-opener deletions `g05`/`g06`. (f) **The fence info rule — LOCATION and VALIDITY** — if the plan's P4 is not scheduled: 29 deletions and 7 phantoms on fill-3's default-reader grid after P1, 13 label errors, and history `d09` (```` ```{.python data-x=" zz"} ```` hides a heading quarto renders); plus P1's residuals after Session 263: VT/FF between the fence run and the tag; quarto's `-1`/`-2` id suffixing; ⚠ **`refs.ts` is not passed the line-0 `{}` propagation** Session 263 modelled in the region scanner — unobservable while the one-word check lets a brace-bearing word through (`{}.x {#lst-pb}` defines with or without it; quarto refuses the unseeded twin, `scratchpad/s263/prop/r1`, `r2`), so P4 must pass it when that check learns pandoc's refusal; and `breakQuartoMd` reads the RAW opener, so a backtick inside a brace tag (```` ```{a`b}.c ````) changes its tick count and quarto skips a later `{mermaid}` cell this model reports (`scratchpad/s263/adv/critic/k03`, phantom cell). readqmd's `*` pattern at every `find` start is unreachable (Session 263's docstart lens). (2) **Readers with no backtick fences are never asked** — `markdown_strict`, `markdown_phpextra`, `-backtick_code_blocks` (`readers/s01`–`s24`); cf. (j) of the item above. (5) **Container continuation** — a list item ends at an unindented line after a blank (`containers/d33`, `d34`), and a list, definition, footnote or quote absorbs a column-0 fence line lazily (`d37`, `e07`, `e08`, `e10`, `e11`, `d42`). (6) **An inline construct spanning lines swallows the opener line** — a refused opener's span or a stray ```` ``` ````, `$…$`, a one-tick span, an inline `<!-- -->`, a multi-line HTML attribute, a pipe-table row (`closers/k31`, `k32`, `k65`, `k66`, `k67`, `critic/r10`, `t02`). (7) **Opaque blocks** — `<pre>`, `<script>`, `<style>`, `<textarea>`, a raw TeX environment, a mid-document YAML block (`critic/r01`–`r05`, `r09`, `y01`, `l02`). (8) **The cells view against quarto's three cell extractors** — jupyter and knitr execute a `{lang}` fence nested in a display fence, which this model's documented rule excludes, and `breakQuartoMd` tracks only column-0 plain fences (`closers/j01`, `j02`, `j04`, `j05`, `j06`).

- [ ] **The YAML key/value-separator grammar is implemented TWICE** (filed Session 148 while shipping ②). `src/core/yaml-context.ts` now exports `isMappingSeparator` + `mappingColonAt` (used by all four diagnostics value paths), while `src/core/project-links.ts` has carried a private `mappingColonIndex` since S80/81 that encodes the same YAML rule for the document-link scanner. They are not identical: `mappingColonIndex` accepts any `/\s/` after the colon where YAML — and `isMappingSeparator` — allow only space/tab, so the private one is slightly over-permissive. Deliberately NOT consolidated in S148: a different feature with its own tests, and a cross-module refactor is plan-mode work (SAFEGUARDS). Cross-referenced in both files' doc comments so neither side rediscovers the duplication. Belongs with the two standing "consolidate the grammar" items below (`refs.ts`/`model.ts` cell-option prefix; `yaml-context.ts`/`model.ts` value slot) — a single grammar-consolidation planning session could take all three.

- [ ] **Separator fix residual: two false-negative classes, both on quarto-REJECTED documents** (filed Session 148 with ②; safe direction, no FP risk). (a) **The no-separator form** — `toc:banana` / `#| echo:banana` makes the document a plain scalar, which `quarto render` rejects (exit 1), and we are now silent. This is the cost the plan explicitly accepted. (b) **`key:: value` on a CLOSED key set** — `project:\n  type:: banana` and `website:\n  page-navigation:: true` are quarto-REJECTED (exit 1, the key `type:`/`page-navigation:` is unknown to a closed set), but we are silent: the VALUE path skips, and the unknown-KEY enumerator (`findProjectConfigKeyLines` → `keySpanAt`, deliberately untouched — plan dragon 3) still splits at the first colon and so sees the KNOWN key before it. Before the fix we flagged these with a wrong-reason message ("Value : true is not valid…"); silence is a strict improvement in correctness but a small loss in coverage. Closing (b) means teaching the KEY enumerator the same separator rule, which ADDS flags and so needs its own FP grounding — a separate slice, not a tidy-up.
