GHSA-v2hh-gcrm-f6hx (host confusion via a literal backslash authority delimiter) + GHSA-4c8g-83qw-93j6 (failed IDN canonicalization). Dependency path is `@vscode/vsce → @secretlint/node → @secretlint/config-loader → ajv@8.20.0 → fast-uri@3.1.2`, i.e. **packaging tooling only — it never ships in the `.vsix`**, so runtime exposure is nil. It landed after Session 108 cleared the previous seven advisories, and it is what the methodology dashboard's "high risk" flag currently reads (health 78/100). `npm audit fix` claims to resolve it; do it the S108 way (deliberate bump, never `--force`) and re-run the full matrix + `vsce package`. Small, self-contained, mostly declarative.

- [ ] **R/Julia leak measurement under `diagnosticMode: "workspace"`** (filed Session 93 at the close of the python fix below; explicitly UNMEASURED). Candidate G's `# type: ignore` mute is python-gated (a `#` is a JS syntax error, and `# type: ignore` is **Pyright/PEP-484-specific** — an R (lintr) or Julia (LanguageServer.jl) server treats it as an inert comment, suppressing nothing). Slice 2 made `{r}`/`{julia}` vdoc minting PASSIVE (see the item below), so merely opening a `.qmd` with such a cell mints and opens its model — and if a real R/Julia server is installed, its diagnostics attach to the vdoc, the identical location-independent tracked-set leak. The confirmed leak is Pylance-only; whether R/Julia leak is **not measured** (no such servers in this repo's `test:lsp` harness). Small, evidence-gated: point at a real R (or Julia) server, drive the forwards under `QMD_LSP_DIAGMODE=workspace`, observe. **The FIX SHAPE (an in-content suppression directive on line 0) is what extends — NOT the string `# type: ignore`:** each server needs its OWN suppression directive if one exists (e.g. a lintr `# nolint` line, a Julia `#src`/pragma — to be researched; some may have none). If none exists, the fallback lever is the vdoc-minting-lifecycle item below — but note its S89-filed "gate vdoc minting on a *registered server*" fix was **REFUTED Session 94** (VS Code registers `r`/`julia`/`python`/`javascript` via built-in language basics, so `getLanguages()`/`needsLanguageExtension` cannot gate on server presence; Learning #105), leaving only the unavoidable-first-mint lifecycle change described there. Do NOT reach for the disposal-timing rewrite the S92 plan §5 rejected.

- [ ] **Format Cell adjacency edge from candidate G's line-0 mute — cell whose fence is at document line 0** (Session 93 review, completeness critic, LOW; in_scope but UNVERIFIED). `buildCellVirtualContent` now puts `# type: ignore` on vdoc line 0; when a `.qmd` opens directly with a `{python}` cell (fence at line 0, no front matter/prose), that comment sits immediately above the body (line 1) with no blank between. Format Cell forwards the cell vdoc to the user's real Python formatter; a formatter that inserts a blank line after a leading module comment could emit an edit at body line 1, which `format-cell.ts` `rangeWithinCell` (`:139`) ACCEPTS (it is a body line of the cell) — inserting a spurious blank into the user's cell. **Unverified and likely a non-issue:** the harness has no Python formatter (Format Cell against a real Python formatter is already documented UNPROVEN, `format-cell.ts:22`), and black specifically does NOT insert a blank after a leading comment (it attaches to the following statement). Pinned by the unit test "when the cell's fence is at line 0, the mute sits IMMEDIATELY above the body". Recheck when a real Python formatter (e.g. `ms-python.black-formatter`) is installed; if a formatter does emit the spurious edit, tighten `rangeWithinCell` to reject a leading-blank-insert at the body's first line, or skip the cell-builder injection when `cell.startLine === 0`.

- [ ] **Residual: transient PARSE/SYNTAX-error diagnostics on a mid-typing cell still leak under `diagnosticMode: "workspace"`** (filed Session 93 at the close of the python fix below). A file-level `# type: ignore` mutes Pyright's *type/name/import* diagnostics (the persistent phantom class the fix targets), but **not** *parse/syntax* errors — Pyright reports those before type-checking. So a vdoc built while the user is mid-typing an incomplete expression (`os.` with a dangling dot, `greet(` with an unclosed paren) still surfaces a transient syntax diagnostic on its `.quarto/vdoc-mit/` path under workspace mode. Directly observed Session 93 (the `test:lsp` completion/signature-help fixtures — `os.`, `greet(` — leak `Expected attribute name after "."` / `"(" was not closed` even WITH the mute). Lesser than the fixed class: transient (clears once the line is syntactically complete) and not the cross-cell/import pollution the item was really about; and it matches Posit's own acknowledged residual (their three-technique approach still leaks, `quarto` #855). No clean in-content fix (`# type: ignore` cannot suppress a parse error); would need the heavy own-a-client/close-the-model path the S92 plan rejected. Low priority — reassess only if users report it.

- [ ] **`{r}`/`{julia}` semantic-token vdoc minting is PASSIVE (no user gesture) — but the S89-filed "cheap fix" is REFUTED; downgraded MEDIUM→LOW, residual harm bounded** (filed Session 89 adversarial review; investigated + corrected Session 94, Learning #105). Since item 16 Slice 2 the semantic-tokens provider forwards EVERY mapped language present, so merely *opening* a `.qmd` with an `{r}`/`{julia}` cell mints one vdoc per language on VS Code's debounced timer (writes a copy of the user's cell source to `.quarto/vdoc-mit/` + opens a model), whereas before Slice 2 (python-only forwarding) such a vdoc existed only on an active hover/completion/format gesture. **The S89-filed fix — "gate `embeddedLanguagesIn`'s targets on the languageId being registered via `needsLanguageExtension`/`getLanguages()`" — does NOT work, proven firsthand Session 94:** VS Code core ships built-in *language-basics* extensions (grammar + config, **NO server**) for `r`, `julia`, `python`, AND `javascript` (`resources/app/extensions/{r,julia,python,javascript}`, v1.128.0), so `vscode.languages.getLanguages()` returns all four in EVERY standard VS Code — extension or not — and `needsLanguageExtension` returns `false` for our whole mapped set. A RED integration test's premise assertion (`!getLanguages().includes("r")`) fired immediately, and the app-bundle inspection confirmed why. **Two facts constrain any correct fix:** (1) the FIRST speculative mint is architecturally *unavoidable* — there is no public API to detect whether a *semantic-tokens provider* exists for a language without handing VS Code a real `file:` document, and a `provideDocumentSemanticTokensLegend` returning `undefined` is NOT a reliable "no provider" signal (S89 measured the built-in JS service returning an undefined legend *transiently while it does have a provider*); (2) the residual harm is *bounded* — the vdoc is gitignored, disposed on document close (`providers/semantic-tokens.ts:111` → `disposeVdocs`), and swept on activation (`extension.ts:73` → `sweepStaleVdocs`). **The only CORRECT fix is a lifecycle change** ("mint speculatively; once a language exhausts its `INCOMPLETE_RETRIES` budget with zero usable streams, dispose its vdoc and stop re-minting it for that document until its cell code changes") — medium-complexity, race-prone (the class the S89/S91 reviews found bugs in), and it still cannot avoid that first write. Given the bounded residual harm, **downgraded MEDIUM→LOW** and left open for a future session that wants the lifecycle fix. The distinct concern "an installed R/Julia SERVER now analyses the vdoc and its diagnostics attach to it" is the **R/Julia-leak measurement item above** (unmeasured — needs a real R/Julia server; `# type: ignore` is Pyright-specific and does not extend). NOT verified firsthand for R/Julia (no such server installed here).

- [ ] **`disposeEpoch` grows unboundedly** — leg (a) only; **leg (b) (the latched `mkdtemp` failure) is FIXED Session 101** (Session 89 adversarial review, two LOWs, both confirmed; this item stays OPEN over leg (a) alone). **(a) STILL OPEN:** `disposeEpoch` is written for EVERY closed text document — including ones that never had a vdoc — and never pruned; it is a `Map<string, number>`, so the leak is small and bounded by the session, but it is unbounded in principle. **(b) FIXED Session 101** (with `:102` — the same `fallbackDirPromise` state; a rejected memo was latched for the whole session, so one transient ENOSPC/EMFILE left completion / hover / go-to-definition / signature help / in-cell outline / Format Cell silently dead on every untitled `.qmd` until a window reload). The filed text ("only ever reset in `disposeAllVdocs`") **understated it**: the reset sat behind `if (fallbackDir !== undefined)`, and the resolved value is assigned only in the `.then()` a rejection never runs — so the memo was cleared on the SUCCESS path only and a rejection was never cleared by *anything*, deactivate included. Fixed at the rejection site (a `.catch` that clears the memo — guarded on still being the same attempt, since `disposeAllVdocs` now also resets it unconditionally and a late rejection could otherwise clobber a newer attempt). Pinned by "retries mkdtemp after a transient failure, rather than latching untitled docs dead" (`test/integration/suite/embedded-vdoc.test.ts`), which induces a REAL ENOENT through the real syscall by pointing `$TMPDIR` at a per-run UUID path — no stub. Break-revert-proven.

- [ ] **Semantic tokens: `self` renders a different colour on a `.qmd` than in a real `.py`** (Session 90 review, LOW — raised as "dropping `selfParameter` is strictly worse than carrying it", and that framing was **refuted**, but the underlying observation is real). Resolved firsthand through VS Code's theme trie in the real default theme (Dark 2026): `.qmd` today shows `self` as **#79c0ff** (MagicPython's `variable.language.special.self.python`); a real `.py` shows **#c9d1d9** (Pylance's own scope rule). **Carrying `selfParameter` bare does NOT reconcile them — it invents a THIRD colour, #ffa657** (`parameter` → `variable.parameter`), matching neither. That is why D4 drops it, and the drop is correct. Closing the cosmetic gap for real would mean contributing Pylance's *exact* scope strings under `language: "quarto"` — a Pylance-versioned table that would silently rot when they rename a scope, in exchange for making `self` match `.py` at the cost of making it *less* distinctive than it is today. Filed, not recommended, and explicitly not a defect in the shipped behaviour.

- [ ] **Semantic tokens: the 23 STANDARD token types in `OUR_LEGEND` are pinned by nothing** (Session 90 review, LOW — a pre-existing test hole this slice neither created nor widened, found by the test-quality lens). 17 of the 23 standard names can be deleted from `OUR_LEGEND` with the entire suite (811 unit / 314 integration) still green: no test asserts that our legend actually *declares* the standard set, only that specific names round-trip. A silent deletion would uncolour those token types across every embedded language, with no test failing. Cheap fix: assert `OUR_LEGEND.tokenTypes` contains the full standard set (the same shape as the existing carried-name pin). Not fixed here — it is outside this slice's blast radius (FM #17) and the fix belongs with a broader legend-coverage test.

- [ ] **Semantic tokens: deleting the last `{python}` cell strands the previous vdoc until the `.qmd` closes** (Session 88 Slice-1 review, LOW). Once the language drops out of `embeddedLanguagesIn` (which replaced `hasCellOfLanguage` in Session 89) the provider returns early, so it never calls `ensureVdoc` and the previously-minted vdoc file (and its open model) survive until `disposeVdocs` runs at close. Bounded, self-healing at close, and swept at the next activation. The fix belongs with the churn item above, since both are about the whole-language vdoc's lifecycle.

- [ ] **Within-session accumulation of superseded cell vdocs during heavy editing** (Session 87 item-18 adversarial review — race + correctness lenses; the past-close leak is FIXED, the within-session churn is the residual). Cell vdocs (outline in-cell symbols, Format Cell) are keyed by absolute `cellStartLine`, so inserting/deleting a line above a cell changes its key and mints a new vdoc; a concurrent same-key double-mint (a hover racing a completion) likewise mints a second file. **The past-close leak is fixed** — `docFiles` now tracks every minted URI per document, so `disposeVdocs`/`disposeAllVdocs` reclaim all of them at document close (proven by the "reclaims a vdoc whose KEY changed" test), and the activation sweep is the cross-session backstop. The residual is that, *while a document stays open*, an actively-edited many-cell document can accumulate stale `.py` files (and open Pylance models — feeding the diagnostics item above) until close. Bounded and self-healing (reclaimed at close + next-session sweep), same order as the old in-memory `InCellSymbolStore` behaviour it replaced. A tighter within-session bound would require the outline provider to reconcile its full current cell-vdoc set on each computation (delete cell vdocs no longer in the live set), which is more surface than this fix warranted. Low priority.

- [ ] **Preview lifecycle: closing a non-`file:` editor (e.g. a Git diff) for a previewed document silently kills the live preview** (surfaced by the Session 84 adversarial review; **PRE-EXISTING**, not introduced by item 15 — it has affected `quarto.preview` since preview shipped; operator-confirmed as deferred rather than fixed in-slice, since `onDocumentClosed` is listed "unchanged" in the item-15 plan's §8 contract and the fix touches lifecycle shared by all three preview commands). `features/preview.ts` `onDocumentClosed` (:281-283) disposes by `doc.uri.fsPath`, **discarding `doc.uri.scheme`**. The built-in Git extension's read-only `git:` diff of a file has the SAME `fsPath` as the working-tree file, so `git:` document-close events reap a live preview for that path. Reproduced end-to-end by two independent reviewers in a real Extension Development Host (VS Code 1.128) against the genuine built-in Git extension: preview `report.qmd`, open its Git diff, close the diff tab → the preview server is killed and the pane disappears, with no error. **Severity: annoying, not dangerous** (no orphan, no corrupt state — the reap path is the normal one, just fired by the wrong document). A faithful fix ignores non-`file:` schemes in `onDocumentClosed` (a preview only ever exists for an on-disk file, so this is safe) or keys sessions by `uri.toString()` rather than `fsPath`; either way it needs its own TDD pass with a regression check across `preview`/`previewFormat`/`previewScript`. Session 84 DID close the same hole in the new `previewScript` **gate** (`uri.scheme !== "file"` — a `git:` diff of a spin script no longer previews the working-tree file), so the remaining exposure is the shared close-handler only. (Adversarial review, Session 84; Learning #92.)

- [ ] **`previewFormat`: nested flow collections in `format:` + a differing-format request dropped during preview startup** (both surfaced/filed by the Session 82 adversarial review, item 15). (1) `core/preview-format.ts` `parseFlowCollection` splits flow collections on commas naively, so a flow collection nested inside another (`format: {html: {a: 1, b: 2}}`) mis-parses the inner comma — astronomically rare in a real `format:` block, degrades to a MISSING name (never a wrong `--to`, since the caller falls back to Quarto's default), same fail-safe class as Slice-1's flow-collection scanner limitation. (2) `features/preview.ts` `openPreview`: a `quarto.previewFormat` request for a DIFFERENT format that arrives WHILE a preview is still starting (the up-to-60s `starting` window) is silently dropped by the `to`-unaware `starting` guard — the reorder-before-dispose fix (Session 82) already converted the nastier teardown-then-nothing outcome into a benign drop-and-keep-the-in-flight-preview, so the residual is only "the user's format switch during startup is ignored; re-run once the preview is up." A faithful fix makes the in-flight reservation `to`-aware (track the pending `to`, restart/queue on a differing format, or surface feedback). Both LOW severity. (Adversarial review, Session 82; Learning #90.)

- [ ] **`_quarto.yml` document-link scanner: flow-collection and pathological-quoted-key value shapes** (surfaced by the Session 80 Slice-1 adversarial review; both confirmed but deferred as fail-safe, documented limitations). `core/project-links.ts` `findPathValueCandidates` reuses the scalar value-token grammar, so a **flow collection** (`chapters: [intro.qmd, part.qmd]`, `opts: {file: x.qmd}`) is emitted as one token containing the brackets — its inner path elements are never split, so a real file inside a flow collection gets no link. Block style (`- intro.qmd`) is idiomatic in `_quarto.yml` and the item-14 plan (§2.5/§5.1) explicitly scoped the scanner to block style, so this was never claimed. Separately, a **quoted key containing `": "`** (a colon *and* a space, e.g. `"a: b": v`) can mis-anchor `mappingColonIndex` (a colon followed by a non-space, like `"a:b":`, is handled correctly; only the space-inside case is not) — astronomically rare in real config, and matches the existing `core/project-yaml.ts` `keySpanAt` naive-colon precedent. **Both degrade to a MISSING link, never a wrong one** (the existence check makes every scanner gap fail-safe). Low priority; if pursued, a faithful fix splits flow-collection elements into per-element candidate spans and makes the quoted-key colon detection quote-aware. (Adversarial review, Session 80; Learning #88.)

- [ ] **Sub-feature (c) — R-package workspace-symbol exclusion** (deferred from item 11's plan §9 Q3, filed Session 71). Since v1.127.0, Posit excludes markdown headers from workspace-symbol search (`Ctrl+T`/`Cmd+T`) by default when the workspace root is an R-package project (a `DESCRIPTION` file present), controlled by a three-way enum setting (`quarto.symbols.exportToWorkspace`: `default`/`all`/`none`, PR #751). Unrelated file (`src/providers/workspace-symbols.ts`/`core/workspace-symbols.ts`), narrower audience (R-package projects specifically), zero shared risk or architecture with item 11's (a)/(b) slices. `DESCRIPTION`-file/R-package detection has no precedent anywhere in this codebase — if pursued, it is new infrastructure, not an extension of existing logic. Low priority, unranked.

- [ ] **Outline show/hide toggle: cosmetic re-expand-on-toggle fix** (deferred from item 11's plan §9 Q4, filed Session 71). The dispose-and-re-register mechanism the toggle uses to force a live Outline refresh (plan §2.4) causes VS Code to rebuild the Outline tree **collapsed** each time the setting changes. Posit's own shipped fix (PR #974) adds an explicit re-expand call afterward to compensate, but the specific VS Code command their fix references was not independently verified this session as a stable, public API — verifying it and wiring the same compensating call is small, self-contained future work. Low priority, cosmetic only (the toggle itself functions correctly without it).

- [ ] **YAML schema diagnostics: an anchored or quoted `project:`/`website:`/`book:` container header silently disables scanning of that whole block** (surfaced by the Session 47 adversarial review; confirmed, both are false NEGATIVES — the safe direction, never a wrong flag — so shipped as a documented gap, not fixed in-session). `mappingContainerKey` (`core/yaml-context.ts`, shared with the 6d-6 nested-completion feature) requires the value after the colon to be empty or comment-only to recognize a line as a pure-mapping container; `project: &p` (a YAML anchor) and `"project":`/`'project':` (a quoted container name) both fail that check, so `findProjectConfigKeyLines` never enters the block at all — no diagnostics from it, ever, even for a genuinely unknown key inside. A leading-BOM false negative in the SAME class was fixed this session (child-key quoting was fixed too, since THAT is a false positive — the feature's core-promise violation); these two remain because fixing them touches `mappingContainerKey`, which is SHARED with the unrelated 6d-6 nested front-matter completion feature — a cross-module change needing its own TDD pass across both consumers (SAFEGUARDS: no cross-module refactor without plan mode). Low priority (anchors/quoted containers are rare in practice; the failure mode is silence, not a false alarm). (Adversarial review, Session 47.)

- [ ] **Embedded-completion secondary-edit filter — defense-in-depth for `command` + divergent primary `textEdit`** (surfaced by the Session 28 6e-1 adversarial review; both refuted 0/3 as out-of-v1-scope). `providers/embedded.ts` `filterOutOfCellEdits` strips out-of-cell `additionalTextEdits` by region membership (the v1 obligation — plan §6 6e-1 DONE #3), but does NOT inspect (a) a post-accept `item.command` (an arbitrary command an embedded server could attach) or (b) a divergent PRIMARY `item.textEdit`/`item.range`. Both are non-issues in v1: `command` is mitigated by `itemResolveCount=0` (the forward omits the 4th arg, and there is no `resolveCompletionItem`), and a divergent primary range is **structurally impossible** to land out-of-cell (VS Code clamps a primary completion range to a single line containing the cursor — vscode.d.ts `CompletionItem.range`). Becomes relevant only if a future 6e slice enables item resolution to surface real auto-imports — then region-check/strip `command` and validate the primary `textEdit` range too. Low priority. (Adversarial review, Session 28.)

- [ ] **Audit `src/providers/embedded.ts`'s existing completion/hover/go-to-def/signature-help forwarding vdocs for spurious global-Problems-panel leakage** (surfaced as a side effect of Session 68's code-cell diagnostics forwarding research, filed Session 69 per that plan's §9 Q4). Posit's own PR history (`quarto-dev/quarto` PR #832, pre-v1.133.0) shows their equivalent pre-existing request-forwarding vdocs were, before an explicit fix, accidentally surfacing spurious entries in VS Code's global Problems panel — a risk inherent to any project using request-forwarding virtual documents, independent of whether diagnostics forwarding is deliberately built (this project chose not to — see item 10, CLOSED Session 69). This project's own equivalent vdocs have never been checked for the same accidental-leakage bug. Small, self-contained, unranked — no known severity yet, since no firsthand check has been done.

- [ ] **`CURATED_EXECUTE_KEYS` hidden-visibility fidelity tuning** (surfaced by the Session 23 6d-6 adversarial review; refuted 0/3, the refuters defended inclusion 3/3). The curated execute children include `enabled`/`daemon`/`daemon-restart`, which carry `hidden:true` in the Quarto 1.7.33 schema — i.e. Quarto's OWN completion suppresses them. Including them is a deliberate, defensible call (`execute:\n  enabled: false` is this project's canonical render-clean idiom, and all three are valid/documented/render-accepted options), but a future session may choose to (a) mirror Quarto's visibility (drop the hidden ones), or (b) strengthen the exact-equality shape-lock test to cross-check the live schema with a `hidden` filter so the curated set can't silently drift from a real schema entry. Pure judgment/test-hardening; no wrong/missing completion today (the names ARE all genuine execute children, verified firsthand). Low priority. (Adversarial review, Session 23.)

- [ ] **Empty value slot abutting a trailing inline comment can glue value to comment** (surfaced by the Session 22 6d-5 adversarial review; refuted 0/3 as out-of-slice-scope, but a real cross-module pattern). When the value slot is EMPTY and a pre-existing inline comment follows (`key:  # c`, cursor before `#`), accepting a value inserts it with no separating space before the comment (`key:  true# c`). Root cause is the shared value-slot grammar: BOTH `core/qmd/model.ts` `slotsOf` (6d-2 cell path) and `core/yaml-context.ts` `topLevelSlots` (6d-5 FM path) compute an empty value span when the slot abuts a comment, and the provider's leading-space-on-`:` heuristic only fires when the slot abuts the COLON, not a comment. Contrived trigger (empty slot + pre-existing comment + cursor exactly before `#`); not a path a normal accept produces. A faithful fix covers BOTH call sites in one pass (suppress value completion when the empty slot abuts a comment, or append a separating space when the next non-space char is `#`) — cross-module, its own TDD pass (SAFEGUARDS: no cross-module refactor without plan mode; FM #18). Low severity. (Adversarial review, Session 22.)

- [ ] **Multi-cursor / multi-selection formatting** (deferred from Phase 7 formatting toggles, Session 14). `features/formatting.ts` toggles only the PRIMARY selection (`editor.selection`); a multi-cursor edit would need to apply N replacements and recompute N resulting selections across shifting offsets. Low priority, its own TDD slice.

- [ ] **Project-wide CLI spawn timeout** (surfaced by the Session 20 6d-3 adversarial review; refuted as a 6d-3-scoped defect, but a real cross-cutting hardening). No `quarto` spawn has a timeout — `quarto/cli.ts` `resolveBinary()` (`--version`, used by render/preview/verify) and the new `quartoSharePath()` (`--paths`, on the completion hot path) both `await execFileAsync(...)` with no `timeout`. A wedged/very-slow binary therefore HANGS rather than degrading: for the schema reader the first `#|` completion stays pending (never reaching the catch→curated fallback) and the cached promise pins it for the session. Low severity (pathological — a healthy `quarto --paths` returns in ~50 ms; VS Code also cancels slow completion providers). A consistent fix adds a `{ timeout }` to ALL CLI spawns in one pass (so the degrade-to-curated/degrade-gracefully guarantee holds under hang, not just under reject) — a deliberate cross-file change, its own TDD/verification pass, NOT a 6d-3 spot-fix (FM #18). (Adversarial review, Session 20.)

- [ ] **Schema-reader cache: retry after a transient first-load failure** (deferred from the Session 20 6d-3 review; refuted as v1-acceptable). `features/yaml-schema-source.ts` caches the load PROMISE once per session (`cached ??= loadSchemaIndex()`); since `loadSchemaIndex` always resolves (curated on failure), a transient first-load failure (e.g. a momentarily-busy CLI) PINS the curated fallback until a window reload, even after Quarto becomes reachable. Low severity (window reload fixes it; first-load failures are rare; documented as an accepted v1 limitation). A faithful fix caches only a SUCCESSFUL parse and retries (debounced) after a fallback, distinguishing "real schema" from "fell back to curated." Its own small TDD pass. (Adversarial review, Session 20.)

- [ ] **`parseSchemaIndex` all-or-nothing degradation across categories (forward-compat)** (surfaced by the Session 21 6d-4 review; refuted 0/3 as v1-acceptable). 6d-4 changed the fallback guard from per-category (`cellFields.length>0 ? … : CURATED`) to joint (`cellFields.length===0 && fmFields.length===0 ? CURATED_SCHEMA_INDEX : indexOf(cellFields, fmFields)`), consistent with replace-semantics (Learning #27). So a future schema that populates one category but EMPTIES the other (e.g. document-* present, all cell-* gone) would yield `[]` for the empty category instead of its curated set. Impossible today (the installed 1.7.33 file has 94 cell + 383 document fields; both always populated) and within the documented-acceptable degradation envelope (`[]` = fewer suggestions, never a false "unknown key"; never throws). A faithful fix would degrade PER-category to the matching curated set — but that mixes real+curated for the empty category, a deliberate divergence from replace-semantics; defer until a real partial-schema Quarto version appears. Trivial. (Adversarial review, Session 21.)

- [ ] **Mid-line HTML comments in `scanRegions`** (deferred from Phase 7 math-preview adversarial review, Session 15; confirmed 3/3 refuters). `core/qmd/model.ts` classifies HTML comments per WHOLE line (`COMMENT_OPEN`/`COMMENT_FULL_LINE`), so a comment that **opens mid-line** (`text <!--`) or **closes mid-line** (`--> $x$`) is mis-handled at the character level. Two symptoms in `core/math-regions.ts`: math inside a mid-line-opened comment is reported (false positive), and real math after a mid-line `-->` is missed (false negative). **This is a shared-model gap — it affects `core/refs.ts` (cross-ref index) identically**, not just math. A faithful fix needs character-level comment tracking in `scanRegions` (interacting with inline code spans + fences) and a full regression across outline/refs/citations — its own TDD pass (SAFEGUARDS: no cross-module refactor without plan mode). Low severity (mid-line comments are uncommon in `.qmd`). Documented in `core/math-regions.ts`'s docstring.

- [ ] **Honest test name for the math-preview "re-render on edit" integration test** (surfaced by the Session 16 diagram-preview review; the identical pattern exists in `test/integration/suite/math-preview.test.ts`). Its only assertion is panel-count, which proves no-stacking, NOT that a re-render occurred (webview content is F5-only residue). Rename the test + comment to what it proves, as was done for the diagram-preview test this session. Trivial, test-only — left untouched in Session 16 to honor FM #8/#18 (another feature's code). (Adversarial review, Session 16.)


- [ ] **Consolidate the YAML value-slot grammar between `yaml-context.ts` and `model.ts`** (surfaced Session 24, 6d-6 cont.). Session 24 extracted `valueSlotAfterColon` in `core/yaml-context.ts` so the top-level AND nested front-matter value-slot grammar (skip leading ws, strip an unquoted trailing inline comment, keep quoted scalars) is ONE implementation. But `core/qmd/model.ts` `slotsOf` (the cell-option `#|` value path, 6d-2) still computes the SAME grammar in its own mirror. The faithful fix routes both through one shared helper — a cross-module change (SAFEGUARDS: no cross-module refactor without plan mode; FM #18), so its own TDD pass. Low severity (the two are byte-identical today; drift risk only). (Session 24, 6d-6 cont.)

- [ ] **De-duplicate the integration `EXTENSION_ID` constant** (surfaced Session 11; now 13 copies). `const EXTENSION_ID = "rmsharp.vscode-quarto-ext"` is copy-pasted into all 13 `test/integration/suite/*.test.ts` files (the Session 28 `embedded.test.ts` added the 13th). When the `publisher` changed this session, every copy went stale at once and RED'd 8 "should be discoverable" failures. Extract to one shared module (ideally derived from `package.json`'s `publisher`+`name`) so an identity change can't silently break discoverability again. Low risk, pure test refactor — its own TDD-exempt pass. (Learning #18.)

- [ ] **Indented (4-space) code blocks as a skip-region** (deferred from Phase 6b adversarial review). `core/qmd/model.ts` `scanRegions` skips YAML front matter, HTML comments, and *fenced* code, but not CommonMark §4.4 *indented* code blocks (a line indented ≥4 spaces after a blank line). So `findBodyLines` emits them and `core/refs.ts` indexes a `{#fig-…}` shown inside one as a **phantom** cross-ref label (verified against `quarto render` → `?@fig-…` unresolved). The faithful fix must not false-skip 4-space **list-item continuation** content (the model tracks no list context), so it needs its own list-aware TDD pass. ~~Low severity (uncommon construct).~~ (Adversarial review B/D, Session 8.) ⚠ **THE "UNCOMMON CONSTRUCT" HALF OF THAT SEVERITY IS CONTRADICTED BY MEASUREMENT** (Session 251's real-document audit, [`docs/REAL-DOCUMENT-TRIAGE.md`](docs/REAL-DOCUMENT-TRIAGE.md); annotated by Session 253). A line indented ≥4 spaces after a blank line occurs in **180 of 3,383 authored `.qmd`/`.Rmd` documents — 5.3%**, the highest construct count of all 192 items audited (detector `scratchpad/s251/det/447.py`, control-calibrated). It is not uncommon. ⚠ **BUT DO NOT READ 180 AS THE SIZE OF THIS DEFECT, AND DO NOT READ THIS ANNOTATION AS A PROMOTION — the item was deliberately KEPT in "Polish / deferred" by the operator at Session 253.** Two reasons the number does not size the item. **(1) WRONG INSTRUMENT:** that audit scored `findHeadings` against `quarto render`, and *this* item describes a **CROSS-REFERENCE** defect — a `{#fig-…}` inside an indented block indexed as a phantom label. The cross-ref surface was never measured, so the defect's real-document incidence is **UNKNOWN**, not 180. The 22 heading-divergent documents this construct co-occurs with are mixed-direction (12 phantom / 8 lost / 2 level), which is what coincidence looks like, not cause. **(2) THE OTHER DEFERRAL REASON IS UNTOUCHED:** the fix still must not false-skip 4-space list-item continuation content, and the model still tracks no list context. ⚠ **What is owed before this is sized:** measure the CROSS-REF behaviour over the real corpus (`scratchpad/s251/realfiles.txt`) the way Session 251 measured headings. Until then "low severity" rests on one premise that is refuted and one that was never tested.

- [ ] **The `🐉1` EPERM discriminator cannot run as root or on Windows, and the whole temp-sweep suite is POSIX-shaped** (found by the Session 103 adversarial review; **the CODE is correct on every platform — this is test reach, not a defect**). `isProcessDead`'s only discriminating test asserts `isProcessDead(1) === false`, which works because `kill(1,0)` throws `EPERM` for a non-root user. Measured S103: **as root `kill(1,0) does NOT throw`, so the shipped and the naive `catch { dead }` implementations become identical and the assertion passes while proving nothing**; on Windows there is no PID 1 at all (NT allocates PIDs in multiples of 4). S103 made the test probe its premise and `skip()` loudly rather than pass vacuously, so it is honest — but that means **🐉1 is UNVERIFIED whenever CI runs as root (containers routinely do) or on Windows**, and the suite would go quietly green. Also unmeasured on those platforms: `process.getuid` is undefined on Windows so G3's uid check is skipped by design; `fs.mkdtemp` applies no POSIX mode there (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp` is per-user, so privacy holds by a different mechanism). **Fix options:** a root-safe discriminator (spawn a child as another uid — needs privileges), or a seam that injects the `kill` probe so the three outcomes (no-throw / EPERM / ESRCH) can be exercised without a real foreign process. **Sizing: LOW** — this project runs its suite on macOS as a normal user, where the discriminator does work; the risk is a future CI change silently disarming the one test that catches the inversion.

- [ ] **Item 17b — Reticulate execution pathway for mixed R/Python Knitr documents** (`quarto.cells.useReticulate`; split from item 17, Session 109). In a Knitr-engine document Posit can route `{python}` cells through R's `reticulate` instead of Jupyter. **Grounded S109:** `reticulate` appears nowhere in `src/`; Run Cell today delegates by language only (`src/core/execution-delegate.ts` `pickDelegate`/`delegateCommandsFor`, Python → Jupyter) with no notion of the document's compute engine. So this needs (1) a config `quarto.cells.useReticulate`, (2) ~~**engine detection (Knitr vs Jupyter) — which does not exist yet and is the load-bearing unknown to scope first**~~ — **SUPERSEDED, Session 165: it exists.** `documentEngineForScoping` (`src/core/document-engine.ts`) now answers knitr/jupyter/markdown/julia for a document from its front matter AND, since S165, from its cell languages the way `markdownExecutionEngine` does. It was built for DIAGNOSTICS scoping, so a router would need to check that its declines (`undefined` on an `.Rmd`, an `{{< include >}}`, an unreadable selector) are acceptable for EXECUTION — where guessing wrong sends a cell to the wrong interpreter rather than merely mis-scoping a squiggle, a different and stricter bar, and (3) a delegate-selection branch that, when engine=Knitr ∧ flag set, sends `{python}` cells down the R pathway. Severity LOW, **narrow audience** (only mixed R/Python Knitr users). ⚠ The "is engine detection worth building for this alone?" gate is MOOT since Session 165 — it is built. What remains to confirm is whether a diagnostics-grade resolver is safe enough to ROUTE execution with.

- [ ] **Notebook-renderer: minor pandoc-fidelity divergences (all LOW, mostly PRE-EXISTING; accepted/documented Session 116).** Surfaced by the S116 adversarial panel, each verified firsthand vs quarto pandoc 3.6.3; none fixed (LOW + pathological or cosmetic). (1) **Apostrophe not entity-escaped** — a `'` in a value renders literally (`title="Don't"`) where pandoc writes `&#39;`; markdown-it's `escapeHtml` deliberately omits `'`. **Cosmetic only — the value sits inside a double-quoted attribute, so the parsed DOM is byte-identical**; no injection. (2) **Empty `::: {}`** falls through unrendered; pandoc emits a bare `<div>` (pinned by an existing test; pointless input). (3) **Fenced div interrupts an open paragraph** (no blank line before `:::`) where pandoc requires the blank line — PRE-EXISTING (`alt:["paragraph",…]` ruler registration since S113/S114, byte-identical old-vs-new), shared with the callout path. (4) **1–3 leading spaces before `:::`** still open a div; pandoc requires column 1 — PRE-EXISTING opening-fence indent handling. (5) **Non-ASCII class/id/key names** (`.класс`) fall through; pandoc accepts Unicode letters — PRE-EXISTING ASCII `\w` grammar. (6) **Escaped separator in an UNQUOTED value** (`key=a\ b`) falls through; pandoc unescapes to `a b` (`unescapeAll` does not handle `\`+space). (7) **A bare `X` and a literal `data-X` in the same div** — the plugin dedups on the post-`data-` name and keeps one; pandoc emits both (a duplicate, invalid-HTML attribute) — the plugin's single-attr output is arguably cleaner. Each is niche; revisit only if a real document hits one. **S118 addendum (custom-title panel, all LOW, all PRE-EXISTING in root cause, each verified firsthand vs quarto render 1.7.33):** (8) **Pandoc heading attributes leak into an extracted title** — `::: {.callout-note}` / `## Heading {#id .cls}` shows title `Heading {#id .cls}` where quarto shows `Heading` (the `{…}` becomes the heading's attributes). Root cause is the PRE-EXISTING markdown-it gap that it does not implement pandoc's header-attribute syntax — the same literal `{…}` also leaks in a body `<h2>` (when `title=` is present so the heading is not extracted); the S118 change only makes it newly visible in the title bar. Fixing = parse pandoc heading attributes (affects all headings, not just titles), out of scope. (9) **`}` inside a quoted callout `title=` flips the callout to a plain generic div** — `::: {.callout-note title="a}b"}` renders `<div class="callout-note" title="a}b">` (no admonition) where quarto renders a titled callout, because `calloutType`'s strict `ATTR_BLOCK` gate `/^\{[^}]*\}$/` rejects any interior `}` while `parseDivAttrs`'s relaxed gate accepts a `}` in a quoted value. PRE-EXISTING (the strict gate since S113/S114); fix = derive the callout type from `parseDivAttrs`'s parsed classes so the two gates agree (a refactor of the byte-identical callout path). (10) **Multi-line setext "heading" extracted as a multi-line title** — `Line one` / `Line two` / `======` inside a callout: markdown-it treats it as a 2-line setext H1 and the extractor lifts it into the title (embedded newline), where pandoc does not treat it as a heading at all (default title, body paragraph). PRE-EXISTING markdown-it-vs-pandoc setext divergence; pathological. (11) **Whitespace-only quoted attribute value renders a callout** — `::: {.callout-note title=" "}` renders a callout with a single-space title where pandoc rejects the whole `:::` line as paragraph text. PRE-EXISTING `tokenizeDivAttrs` leading-space quirk affecting all quoted values (generic divs too). **S119 addendum (collapse panel, both LOW, both PRE-EXISTING in root cause, each verified firsthand vs quarto render 1.7.33):** (12) **A bare `collapse` flag (no `=value`) on a callout renders a plain non-collapsible callout** where quarto rejects the whole fenced-div block as literal `:::` text (invalid pandoc attribute syntax → no callout at all). Root cause is the PRE-EXISTING `calloutType`-vs-`parseDivAttrs` leniency: `calloutType`'s `ATTR_BLOCK` regex matches `.callout-note` even when `tokenizeDivAttrs` rejects the block, so `attrs` is `null`, the collapse (and title) paths are never reached, and the callout renders plain/default. Any invalid bareword inside a callout block (`{.callout-note xyz}`) manifests it identically; byte-identical pre-collapse-change. Same fix as divergence (9): derive the callout type from `parseDivAttrs`'s parsed classes so the two gates agree. Out of scope. (13) **A quoted collapse value with LEADING whitespace** (`collapse=" true"`) renders an expanded-collapsible callout where quarto emits the whole `:::` line as literal text. PRE-EXISTING leading-space handling — the SAME root as divergence (11); a `title=" Hi"` behaves identically (plain callout for us, literal text in quarto), an upstream fence-recognition property unchanged by the collapse work (trailing-only `"true "` and interior `"a b"` both parse in quarto and match us). Out of scope. ⚠ **Session 262:** (a) with a duplicate `collapse=`, quarto applies the FIRST (`scratchpad/s262/critic/render2.json`), contradicting `calloutCollapse`'s comment ("the last wins"); (b) adopting the attribute-tokenizer plan's browser-safe leaf would fix sub-items 5, 6, 9 and 11; (c) whether quarto's readqmd pre-passes apply to `.ipynb` markdown cells is unmeasured — measure it before adopting.
  - **S117 addendum (nested manifestation of (2) and (3)):** the S117 adversarial panel flagged that when an empty `::: {}` (divergence 2) or a paragraph-glued `::: {.inner}` (divergence 3) appears NESTED inside another div, the depth counter mis-pairs (empty `{}` is not counted as an opener since the plugin does not render it as a div; a glued opener IS counted since the plugin's `alt:["paragraph"]` registration interrupts paragraphs). Adjudicated FIRSTHAND (pre-fix vs post-fix vs pandoc): **both are consequences of the pre-existing standalone divergences here (2)/(3), NOT of the S117 depth fix** — for same-length fences the empty-`{}` case is byte-identical pre-fix and post-fix, and the glued case is now MORE self-consistent post-fix (the interrupted inner div is contained by the outer, where pre-fix left it orphaned). Because `isDivOpener` is deliberately consistent with what the plugin actually renders as a div, making it match pandoc's broader opener set (empty `{}`, trailing-colon `::: {.x} :::`, degenerate literal-brace fallbacks) would DE-sync depth from the rendered structure — so these stay out of scope until the standalone divergences (2)/(3) are themselves addressed. Still LOW/pathological.

- [ ] **`docs/POSIT-COMPARISON.md` understates notebook-renderer parity (PRE-EXISTING, LOW).** Lines ~651–652 still assert "This project has no equivalent notebook-renderer contribution." — stale since S113 (when `contributes.notebookRenderer` `quarto-mit.markdown-it-callout` first shipped), NOT caused by the S122 box-CSS fix; surfaced by the S122 completeness-lens sweep over 17d. The project now ships a `notebookRenderer` extending `vscode.markdown-it-renderer` that renders Quarto callouts + generic fenced divs in `.ipynb` markdown cells (S113–S122). Fix = update that parity row to note the contribution (narrower scope than Posit's full-markdown renderer, but no longer "no equivalent"). Doc-only, no logic → TDD-exempt, its own small session. Filed rather than fixed in S122 to hold 1-and-done / avoid "while I'm at it" scope creep.

### Enumeration-only — measured at zero reach into authored documents (Session 251 audit)

*The items below were moved here from "Up Next" by **Session 252**, on the measurement in
[`docs/REAL-DOCUMENT-TRIAGE.md`](docs/REAL-DOCUMENT-TRIAGE.md). Each one describes a construct
that appears in **0 of 3,383 authored `.qmd`/`.Rmd` documents** — the corpus of documents a human
actually wrote (`scratchpad/s251/realfiles.txt`), as distinct from the 60,998 generated probe
documents this project had been sweeping, whose median document is 51 bytes.*

***What this claim is.** Each item has a detector in `scratchpad/s251/det/`, calibrated against a
known-positive control drawn from the item's own witness — `scan.py` refuses to print a count for
a detector that does not fire on its control, because a broken detector's zero is
indistinguishable from a true one. Every zero was then re-attacked by a second agent forbidden the
original detector; that pass overturned 6 verdicts elsewhere, and upheld all 90 of these.*

***What this claim is NOT.** It is **construct absence in one corpus**, not a finding that the item
is wrong. These are mostly correct observations about constructs pandoc genuinely supports. The
corpus is one machine's — 3,383 files but only 2,026 distinct basenames, weighted toward R-centric
scientific writing — so "zero here" is not "zero anywhere". Nothing has been deleted and no item's
own text was altered.*

***⚠ The detector filenames are POSITIONAL, and the base commit is not the obvious one.**
`det/52.py` is keyed to the item's `BACKLOG.md` line number **as of `b85626f3`** — Session 250's
close-out, the state the audit read — NOT as of the audit's own close-out `b50e5f6b`, which had
already shifted every line below 52 by two when it filed the panel-tabset item. Resolve one with
`git show b85626f3:BACKLOG.md`, never against the current file. ⚠ This matters more than it looks:
items here sit exactly two lines apart, so resolving against the wrong commit lands on a DIFFERENT
item that is still a valid item start — it fails silently. Session 252 walked into exactly that and
was caught only by cross-checking each resolved line against its recorded title. This is the
positional-citation hazard in this file's own header, met in practice.*

***To promote an item back to "Up Next":** show the construct in a document someone wrote — either
by widening the corpus or by refuting the detector — and move it, noting what you found. That is a
lower bar than it sounds: Session 251's own adversarial pass cleared it six times.*

- [ ] **`FRONTMATTER_CLOSE` accepts `...` as a front-matter terminator and quarto REFUSES TO RENDER such a document** (found by Session 177 while mirroring `breakQuartoMd`'s delimiter rule; **verified firsthand**; **PRE-EXISTING**; LOW). YAML's own document-end marker is legal YAML but not legal quarto: `---`/`title: t`/`...` renders `ERROR: Expected YAML front matter to end with '---'` from `validateDocumentFromSource` and produces no output at all. Our `FRONTMATTER_CLOSE` is `/^(?:---|\.\.\.)[ \t]*$/`, so `scanRegions` closes the block there and every front-matter consumer (`findFrontMatter`, `inFrontMatter`, `frontMatterContentLines`, the front-matter value enumerators) models a document quarto will not build. NOT a cardinal false positive in the usual sense — quarto does not ACCEPT the document either — which is why this is LOW rather than urgent, and it is why the oracle scores such a row `unrelated` (nonzero exit, no validation text) and learns nothing from it. S177's new region scanner already matches quarto here (it closes on `---` only), so the two now disagree with each other inside our own codebase — that disagreement is the reason to file it rather than leave it implicit. ⚠ **ENUMERATION-ONLY (S251): 0 of 3,383 authored documents** — detector `scratchpad/s251/det/309.py`. - [ ] **Raw `` ```{=html} `` / `` ```{=latex} `` blocks ARE validated by quarto, and we still enumerate none of them** (filed by the Session 160 §9 review's fp-cardinal lens as unverified; **GROUNDED FIRSTHAND BY SESSION 172 — the claim holds, and the mechanism is now known**; **PRE-EXISTING**, lost-TP direction). Quarto's cell recognizer captures the language as `([=A-Za-z]+)` and `=` is INSIDE the class, so a leading `=` is a legal cell LANGUAGE: `breakQuartoMd` builds an ordinary code cell for `` ```{=html} `` whose language is `=html`. Measured vs 1.7.33: `{=html}` + `#| echo: banana` renders **exit 1**, as do `{=latex}`, `{=banana}` and even the degenerate `{=}`; the valid `#| echo: false` renders exit 0, so it is the VALUE. **And the scope is the DOCUMENT ENGINE's, not a reduced one** — `#| cache: banana` (knitr-only) in a raw block renders exit 0 under `engine: markdown` and **exit 1** in a knitr document, exactly like a real cell; `=html` misses quarto's handler list, so `parseAndValidateCellOptions` keeps the engine schema. The comment char is `#` (a `//|` line renders exit 0), and any column-0 `#|` line is read as cell YAML — `#| a comment in html?` renders exit 1 with `YAML value ... must be an object`. **S172 deliberately did NOT adopt it.** Its `CELL_INFO` keeps the letter-led rule, so this stays a lost TP — the safe direction. Adopting it WIDENS what we squiggle onto a whole new block class (the cardinal sin) and hands `cell.lang === "=html"` to the outline, the virtual-document language map, run-cell, the crossref index and `cellOptionScopeFor` at once, so it needs that consumer work as its own deliverable. ⚠ **The guard is the `FP GUARD` pin in `test/unit/cells.test.ts` and its twin in `test/unit/yaml-value-flags.test.ts`** — a mutant adopting quarto's grammar verbatim kills those two and NOTHING else in 1583 tests. Oracle row `S172 {=html} raw block + echo (KNOWN residual, lost TP)`. MEDIUM-LOW (real lost TPs, but only on raw blocks carrying cell options). ⚠ **ENUMERATION-ONLY (S251): 0 of 3,383 authored documents** — detector `scratchpad/s251/det/339.py`. Downgraded from Up Next by Session 252; see the section note above.

S159 unquoted the LEAF key on both `.qmd` front-matter surfaces, but the intermediate CONTAINER key is still compared with its quotes attached, so a quoted container silently disables the block beneath it. Grounded firsthand vs quarto 1.7.33: (a) `"execute":` / `  echo: banana` renders **exit 1** with `Field "echo" has value banana` yet we are silent — `mappingContainerKey` (`src/core/yaml-context.ts`) returns the container key WITH quotes, and `nestedParentPath` feeds that raw string to both the `NESTED_CONTAINERS` gate and the `format`-root check; (b) `format:` / `  "html":` / `    code-fold: banana` renders **exit 1** on `code-fold` and we are silent — the quoted format NAME degrades the per-format option set (the review measured 170 fields → 70). The review also reports the same container hole on the `_quarto.yml` VALUE surface (the already-filed S47 container item covers only the KEY surface) — not re-verified firsthand by S159. Note `mappingContainerKey` is SHARED with the 6d-6 nested-completion feature, so this is a cross-consumer change: its own slice with its own TDD pass (SAFEGUARDS), and it is the same defect class as the already-filed "anchored or quoted `project:`/`website:`/`book:` container header" item below — worth doing together. ⚠ **ENUMERATION-ONLY (S251): 0 of 3,383 authored documents** — detector `scratchpad/s251/det/341.py`. Downgraded from Up Next by Session 252; see the section note above.

- [ ] **Quoted-KEY on the `#|` cell-option surface — a lost TP that is BLOCKED on the cell arm-token grammar** (verified firsthand S159; the fix was implemented, then deliberately REVERTED — see `CHANGELOG.md`, Session 159). `#| "echo": banana` renders quarto **exit 1** with `Field "echo" has value banana` exactly as the bare form does, and we are silent — the same lost TP S159 fixed on both front-matter surfaces. Unquoting the cell key ALONE is not shippable: `findCellOptionLines` derives its continuation-guard arm token from `m[4].indexOf(":")` (the FIRST colon, not the real separator), so an option key containing a colon (`#| a:b: "text`, and the `|` block-scalar variant) disarms the guard and the folded line below is emitted as a real option — on documents quarto renders **exit 0**. That FP is already live with a BARE folded key (`#| a:b: "text` / `#| echo: banana"` — verified firsthand, flagged identically pre- and post-S159); unquoting merely widens it to quoted folded keys, so S159 fixed the front-matter twin in the shared `mappingColonAt` and reverted the cell half rather than widen an FP. **Do the arm-grammar fix FIRST** (make the cell arm use the real separator, skipping a quoted key — the same rule `mappingColonAt` now applies), **then** the unquote. `src/core/qmd/model.ts` is deliberately import-free (`yaml-context.ts` imports IT), so sharing the rule needs the cross-module grammar consolidation tracked under "Polish / deferred" — this is the concrete, grounded motivation for that item. ⚠ **ENUMERATION-ONLY (S251): 0 of 3,383 authored documents** — detector `scratchpad/s251/det/343.py`. Downgraded from Up Next by Session 252; see the section note above.

- [ ] **Quoted keys diverge in three non-diagnostic readers and in YAML completion** (reported by the Session 159 §9 review's missed-sites lens; **NOT independently re-verified by S159 — treat the specifics as review-reported, ground before acting**). The lens reports that `src/core/frontmatter.ts`'s `/^bibliography:/` and `src/core/preview-format.ts`'s `/^format[ \t]*:/` match a BARE key literal, so a quoted spelling (`"bibliography": refs.bib`, `"format":`) silently disables bibliography discovery and declared-format parsing respectively — on documents quarto honors normally. ⚠ **The third reader the lens named, `core/refs.ts`'s cell-label scan, is CLOSED (Session 301, measured):** `//| "label": fig-a` and `#| 'label': fig-a` both render `Figure 1`, and the index now reads them (`refs.ts` Source 2 compares the key with one matching pair of quotes stripped). It also reports that completion offers no value items after a quoted key and nothing at all inside a quoted container. All are false negatives (silence), never wrong output. LOW; a natural companion to the container item above since they share the "compare a key literal without unquoting" root. ⚠ **ENUMERATION-ONLY (S251): 0 of 3,383 authored documents** — detector `scratchpad/s251/det/345.py`.
